first_appearance: 2
---

## Appearance

Sixteen and built like a gate hinge: tall for a clay boy, narrow, with slip dried to the colour of old bone up both forearms and a permanent bend in his left little finger from a splinter off the wheel when he was eleven. He cuts his hair short so Ash cannot nest in it, which is a thing the district notices and he never explains. His eyes do not finish sentences; they go to the door, to the fire, to the wheel, and come back without reporting. He smells of damp clay, tallow and the sweetish edge of lead glaze carried up from High Kiln on a bad wind. The thing people notice first is his hands, which are the hands of a thrower and have been working since he was nine. The thing they notice last is that he never looks at Sella when she speaks to him, though he obeys her instantly.

## Voice & Manner

Short, flat, declarative. He asks almost no questions, and when he does it is the tool rather than the clay: *Is it dry? Did you fire it? Who wrote it?* He speaks to the wheel and to the pots as other men speak to animals, and only then to people. When he is angry he gets quieter, the way Sella does, and the household has learned to read the lowering of his voice as a weather signal. He never says *with* or *against* when *I haven't said* will do.

- “The wheel doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady.”
- “You want me to say it. I'm not saying it.”
- “Sella. Don't feed him from your own dish. He's not Vail.”
- “A slate burns in four minutes. Four. I've timed it.”
- “I didn't say I was with them. I said I wasn't against them. That's different. In this district it isn't, but it's what I said.”
- “He's not Vail.” --- said in the kiln-house at first light, refused an answer, and sealed by the boy's arrival.
- “You'll do.” --- to the pot on the wheel, in a voice with no welcome in it and no unkindness either.
- “It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady.” --- to the wheel before anyone, said again on Day 140 of a pot he told *You'll do* while keeping its full note from a High Kiln kiln-master who said it was singing before its time.

## History

He is the son of Garl and Sella Vail, and he took the wheel at thirteen when Garl died in the Long Cold and did not come back and nobody came to draw him. The wheel had been Garl's for twenty years and the seat was worn hollow; Jorra has spent three years trying to fill that hollow with his own weight. He has thrown pots since he was nine, fired them since eleven, and knows the full note of a Vail pot before it leaves his hands, though he will not say so in front of the kiln-masters, who would call it early and make something of it. His house has remembered the Ash Rising since Y.H. 668, when children were taken and the rising was put down by a man with a list; the memory is kept not in stories but in the way certain doors are never closed and certain names are never entered. Since he was fourteen men from the Broken Slates have come into the back room of the kiln-house and spoken in low voices. He has listened to all of them and told none of them yes.

He watched Enna, his sister, die of the second chill at four, and has never forgiven the fire for being warm while she cooled. He watched his father die and was sent to find a collector and could not cross the ford, and has never asked whether he was sent too late or whether he was sent at all. When Corin arrived he said one sentence — *He's not Vail* — and has said nothing about it since, which in that house is a form of argument.

## Wounds & Fears

A father who went uncollected, a sister who went cold in a warm room, and a wheel he was given before he was ready. He fears Sella will die with something unsaid and leave him holding the house and the lie together. He fears the Broken Slates will decide for him what he is, because he has not decided and the longer he waits the more their decision looks like his own. He fears the boy most of all, because the boy hears things. His contradiction: he is the most careful keeper of the Vail fire in four generations and he is quietly helping the people who want to burn every slate in Sill. He has never once thought these two facts cannot live in the same man.

## Desires

He wants the wheel to stay in the house and the fire to hold and nobody to write the boy down as Vail without asking him first. He thinks he wants Sella to say that he has done well with the wheel. What he needs is to learn that burning a slate does not return a person to you, and that the anger he has been nursing for three years is not his alone — it has been lent to him by men who intend to collect on it.

## Bonds


**Ch 117 (bonds).** On Ilmen 8 and 9 afternoons he kept the wheel-shed edge and the household straightened around his silence without a word; offstage on Ilmen 10. He came through the finger's-width door with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust white in a fist-crease, not the bone-coloured slip, parted his dusty forearms from clean as clot from vessel and spoke to the wheel and a pot before any person. He asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate; he knew the full note of a Vail pot three turns ahead and withheld it from any High Kiln master who would call it early, and put his bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, taking it out without the extra holding of Ash 26 — one piece of iron with the holding and the taking and no Grathic word covering both. On Ilmen 9 a wrong wind off High Kiln carried the sweet edge of lead glaze over the boards and out again on the wind that brought it, a direction arrived nowhere with no hand or name under it; the boy filed it as weather beside other greens with a finger between, and Jorra gave it no word. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise under the shed stayed neither given nor withdrawn, gathering interest against the burned slate at five and the four directions, neither called nor paid. No new scar or possession, no change of voice.
**Ch 116 (bonds).** On Ilmen 5 and 6 afternoons he kept the wheel-shed edge and the household straightened around his silence without a word; offstage on Ilmen 7. On the morning of Ilmen 6, before first grey, three or more men unnamed went low into the room off the wheel-shed, keeping its finger's width of open and its sound of low voices, and he gave them no name and no word that the room was cold. He came through the finger's-width door with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and white in a fist-crease where a hand had opened, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; he kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and parted them as clot from vessel, asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, while the broken ones under the lowest rack stayed entered by nobody (T035/T031/T062 advanced, not paid). He spoke to the wheel and a pot before any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note three turns ahead with air under it and a want in it and withheld the knowing from any High Kiln master who would call it early and sing a pot before its time. His bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, took the cold out of the fingertip, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and was left standing so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot; he came out without leaving the extra holding of Ash 26 a second time — one piece of iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no Grathic word covering both mercies. That day, from the basket's low angle, a man from Slate Row whose name was on no roll stood at the yard's edge with his hands loose; the boy filed the face on its own tray and did not join the waiting to the promise under the shed or to the burned slate at five. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name was neither given nor withdrawn while the burned slate stood at five, the shut-room tellings at two, the row at five askings with no witness, the well-saying at four shapings and the Ash and Slate directions apart and unspent. Word of the man at the edge brought no loosening of it. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar, wound, rank or possession and no change of voice.

**Ch 115 (bonds).** On Ilmen 2 and Ilmen 3 afternoons he kept the wheel-shed edge and the household straightened around his silence without a word from anybody; nothing from the turned year, the twelve arithmetics, the reported landing or Tarn's degree toward it came under the shed as sign, lesson, mending, cause, payment or payer, and no word was given under it. He came through the finger's-width door with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and white in a fist-crease, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; three or more unnamed men went low into the back room before first grey and got no name from him and no word that the room was cold. Dusty forearms and clean ones were kept on one tray and parted as clot from vessel; he asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate while the broken ones under the lowest rack stayed entered by nobody. He spoke to the wheel and a pot before any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — and told the pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness, knowing the full note it would give three turns ahead, with air under it and a want in it, and withholding that knowing from any master come up from High Kiln who would call it early and sing a pot before its time; the knowing went on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them. His bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, took the cold out of the fingertip, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and was left standing so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around the waiting — one piece of iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, no Grathic word covering both mercies — and he came out without leaving the extra holding of Ash 26 a second time, gathered once and not made again, for a second would be a habit and a third a way and both want a payer where none stood. With Corin nothing changed: the boy lay under the roof unwritten, *he's not Vail* stayed sealed as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years, and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn while the burned slate stood at five, the shut-room tellings at two, the row at five askings with no witness, the well-saying at four shapings and the Ash 5 joining and the Ash 17, Ash 23 and Stone 23 directions apart and unspent. Word came up on Ilmen 3 that the soft-cuffed clerk was still set at the out-of-line landing below the north bridge with his slate turned inward for a measure nobody called, and that Tarn's head had gone a degree toward it and back to the broom without a word; the boy kept Tarn's degree-turn and Jorra's promise on two places with a finger between and did not lay them together to make a cause, for a doctrine is an account that has found a payer and none stood. Offstage on Ilmen 4. No bond paid, sold, named or spoken; no new scar, possession or change of voice.

He made no song of it, named it to nobody, did not look toward the basket, gave no name to the men in the back room and did not say the room was cold. The boy took the interval apart by what was there and by what was not and filed it on a place of its own with a finger between it and the five held objects, the pot-crack resting, the hand-at-the-sill breath and his own gaps: a private keeping gathered once and not spent, expressly not a sixth held object, not a seventh, not an eighth of anything, not a habit, not a way, not a hearing, not a near-full answer, not a mending, not a cause, not a payment and not a rule, with no payer standing under it. It gathers toward the far-off hand that will one day lay the district's private keepings together, and that hand is not his. The boy did not join it to Jorra's dust, the damper's double bite, the uncalled debt, the covered grain, the charcoal, Ilva's strip or the Ash 23 direction; the edges came near to touching and the finger went between. On Ash 27 he took his finger out and left the interval where he had put it and did not make it again, for a second would be a habit and a third a way and both want a payer standing under them and none stood; he found it where it had been put and left it there, and nobody checked whether anybody had noticed. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn while the claim went on gathering interest against it; the burned slate stood at five, the shut-room tellings at two, the row at five askings and the Ash 5, Ash 17 and Ash 23 directions held apart with a finger between every pair. Three or more men went low into the back room before first grey on each of the three mornings and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold. It cares if they're steady* — and told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness either; he knew the full note that pot would give three turns ahead, with air under it and a want in it, and withheld the knowing from any High Kiln master who would call it early and make a song of it, keeping it where nobody could make a song of it. He read one piece of iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no Grathic word for both mercies. **On Ash 5, when an ownerless voice over the boards joined the burned slate for the first time to the room with the damper shut, he grew quieter and the household straightened around the quiet without an order from anybody; the burned slate still stood at five with a finger between the fifth and the four and no witness standing, the shut-room tellings at two and no third, and the claim went on gathering interest against his promise, which was neither given nor withdrawn.** *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years. With Tarn the old equality held, neither asking about the back room or the ear, his finger leading at the damper while Tarn's stroke went over the flagstones without the half-check. No bond was paid or spoken, no new scar or possession and no change of voice.

**Ch 93 (bonds).** Sella's unfinished sentence still arrived at the pot through Ash 1, 2 and 3: before she had finished telling him the fire was low his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper, took the cold out of the fingertip, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and was left standing, and the household straightened around his silence without a word from either of them. He came through the finger's-width door off the wheel-shed each day with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and white in a fist-crease where a hand had opened, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; he parted dusty forearms from clean as clot from vessel and asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, while the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack stayed entered by nobody. He spoke to the wheel before any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — and told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness either. He knew the full note that pot would give three turns ahead, with air under it and a want in it, and withheld the knowing from any High Kiln master who would call it early and make a song of it, keeping the knowing where nobody could make a song of it. Three or more men went low into the back room before first grey on each of the three days and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold; the shut-room tellings stayed at two with no third. He read one piece of iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no Grathic word for both mercies and no slate entered. He grew quieter whenever the burned-slate word went around and nobody gave him an order to stop; the telling stood at five with a finger between the fifth and the four, no witness standing, no hand named at any flame and no sixth due on a day that had not earned one, and the claim went on gathering interest against his promise. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. With Tarn the two silences under the roof stayed side by side and unjoined; neither asked about the ear or the room. The sixth opening at the kiln mouth on Ash 2 came and went without a word from him and no seventh was made due; six arithmetics stood behind one unsigned figure in Berr's box and nothing of the reading changed anything at the shed. He wants the knowing kept where nobody can make a song of it. No bond changed hands, no name given, no new scar or possession and no change of voice.

**Ch 90 (bonds).** Sella's unfinished sentence still arrived at the pot on the twenty-second and twenty-fourth while his bent finger stood in the cold damper, and the household straightened around his silence; he did not look toward the bed or answer with a word, and she asked nothing about the men, the dust or the damper. With Corin nothing changed: the boy lay under the roof unwritten, *he's not Vail* was sealed, and the promise of his name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn while the burned slate stood at five with a finger between the fifth and the four and no sixth due on a day that had not earned one. **Ch 86 (bonds).** Nothing changed hands with Jorra through Bone 10–12. He kept the wheel and the household's shape at the shed's edge, parted dusty forearms from clean as clot from vessel, spoke to the wheel and a pot before any person and withheld the note known three turns ahead from a High Kiln master who would call it early. His bent finger stood in the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence; the men who went low into the back room before first grey got no name and no word that the room was cold. He grew quieter at the fifth burned-slate telling and the household straightened around the quiet without an order, the claim gathering interest against a promise he neither paid nor refused. Sella's reading went up the road and back without changing anything under the wheel-shed, and Tarn's stroke went over the bright patch without the half-check while Jorra's finger stood where it stood. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name was neither given nor withdrawn. No name given, no debt paid, no bond paid, sold, named or spoken.

**Sella Vail** — his mother. He believes she took Corin in to replace Enna and to keep the house's standing after Garl died. She has never contradicted him. Every obedience he gives her is therefore double: it is love and it is a correction neither of them will make. *Ch 2:* she announced the taking and he answered *he's not Vail*; she got quieter and gave him no answer at all. He went on working, did not look at her, and shifted the damper before she had finished telling him to.

**Corin** — the foundling. Jorra was the first in the house to notice that the boy turns his head toward a pot before anyone taps it, and that he does it to hear the note, not the noise. He has told nobody. He resents the boy for being given a bed in the house while the clay-bound hand sleeps in the loft, and he cannot stop watching him. *Ch 2:* the foundling arrived under the roof and nobody wrote him as Vail, which is what Jorra wanted and what he cannot bear. He spoke to the wheel, to the pots and once to Tarn about a rasp out of its place, and never addressed the boy.

**Alda Reed** — attended Enna's death and Corin's birth, and has never met Jorra's eye for longer than a moment. He knows she wrote something down the night Enna died and has never asked to see it, because asking would make it a thing between them.

**Tarn** — the Vail's clay-bound hand. Jorra treats him as a brother when the wheel needs him and as a servant when it does not. Tarn has decided to hate him for it and keeps failing, because Jorra once sat up all night with him through a fever and has never mentioned it. *Ch 2:* Tarn was given the loft while the foundling was given the bed, and said so in one sentence to Sella. Jorra said nothing; he has never defended the household's arithmetic and never contests it.

**Berr Clay, the Thane of Clay** — Jorra threw a stone at his door in Y.H. 810 and denied it. The Thane suspects the boy is with the Broken Slates and lets him be, which Jorra finds more frightening than being watched.

**Ch 4.** Sella's hand went to the mark before the kiln and not at the bank; he shifted the damper before she had finished and again at dusk, and did not take up her silence about the back room. The boy was at the edge of his notice as a thing that hears: he spoke to the pots and not to him, and came in from the back room with slate dust worked into his forearms --- grey and fine, not slip --- and said *the back room's cold* to nobody. He wanted the boy outside the name Vail and the hollow seat his, and did not say either.

**Ch 5.** The wheel took the house into its rhythm again with slip dried to old bone up both forearms and no slate dust on Stone 13 or Stone 15; the boy noticed the absence and could not ask which it meant. He told a pot *you'll do*, told the wheel it did not care whose hands were on it, and shifted the damper before Sella had finished telling him the fire was low. He did not say the back room was cold. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as it had been on the hour it was spoken, and the foundling went on being carried under the roof without a Vail name, which is what Jorra wanted and what he could not bear.

**Ch 6.** On all five days he came in from the shed with bone-coloured slip dried to old bone up both forearms and the bent little finger, and no slate dust; the boy noticed the absence and could not ask what it meant. He told a pot *you'll do* and *the shoulder's thick*, told the wheel it did not care whose hands, only if they were steady, and moved the damper before Sella had finished telling him the fire was low, going in with the bent finger ahead of his hand. He did not say the back room was cold and did not look toward the basket or the bed. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed; the foundling went on being carried under the roof unwritten.

**Ch 7.** On Stone 23, after five mornings of clean bone-coloured slip, he came in from the back room with fine grey slate dust worked into the creases of his forearms past the bend of the little finger and said nothing about where he had been; the boy read the arranged face the way he had once read a resident who kept his eyes on a chart. He told the pot she would want a thinner slip, told the wheel it did not care whose hands, only if they were steady, and went to the damper before Sella had finished, the bent finger going in first. On Stone 25 the forearms were clean again and he kept dusty and clean as two entries; on Stone 29 the sweeping talk made him quieter and the yard straightened around it without an order. He knows the full note of a Vail pot before it leaves the wheel and would not say so in front of a kiln-master, who would call it early and make something of it; the boy knows this about him as he knows the bent finger, and keeps both where questions can last for years. He did not say the back room was cold and did not look toward the bed; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed through the year's last market.

**Ch 8.** Through the Five the wheel-shed gave him bone-coloured slip dried to old bone up both forearms and the bent little finger leading at the damper; he moved the damper before Sella had finished and did not say the back room was cold. No slate dust came after the Last Day and he never mentioned the back room; he did not look toward the bed or the basket. On the Last Day the grey in the corners was still where it lay and the baking went on around it, and he went quieter as the sweeping talk went around. On Ilmen 1 he worked the slip toward the thaw and the pits with the same forearms and the same silence, and the foundling went into the new year carried under the roof without a Vail name.

**Ch 9.** On Ilmen 4 he came in from the back room with fine grey slate dust worked into the creases of his forearms past the bend of the little finger and said nothing about where he had been; on the other mornings the forearms carried bone-coloured slip, and the boy kept the two states apart. He told a pot it would do, told the wheel it did not care whose hands, and went to the damper before Sella had finished, the bent finger going in ahead of his hand. He knew the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master. He agreed with nobody about the thaw and contradicted nobody, and his silence went around the yard as its own weather signal. He did not look toward the basket and did not say the back room was cold.

**Ch 10.** He comes in from the wheel-shed with bone-coloured slip dried to old bone up both forearms and no grey slate dust worked into the creases past the bend of his little finger on the days the boy watches; on Ilmen 11 the bent finger goes in ahead of his hand at the damper and he moves it before Sella has finished telling him the fire is low, so she shuts her mouth on the instruction and gives it to the pot. He tells a pot *You'll do* and tells the wheel it does not care whose hands are on it, only if they are steady, and does not say the back room is cold. He does not look toward the basket or the bed, has not been told about the woman in the brook and has not asked. Garl's proverb still goes to the pot and his own unfinished sentence still goes nowhere; *he's not Vail* stays sealed through seven more days.

**Ch 11.** He came in from the wheel-shed on all three days with slip dried to the colour of old bone up both forearms and no grey worked past the bend of his little finger, the bent finger going in ahead of his hand at the damper each time, moved before Sella had finished telling him the fire was low. He told a pot *You'll do* and the wheel what it had been told a hundred times --- that it did not care whose hands were on it, only if they were steady --- and said nothing of the room off the shed, whose door kept its finger's width of open. He did not say the back room was cold and did not look toward the bed or the basket. Garl's salt-stiff coat kept a broader man's shape on its peg, the hollow seat its twenty-year lean, and the clean dark line where the ridge had turned was not swept, because a line left where it is is still a line kept. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as on the hour it was spoken.

**Ch 12.** On Day 50 he came in from the room off the shed with grey slate dust past the bent finger and said nothing, and Sella did not ask; the dust went on a tray of its own beside the clean slip of the other mornings. He told the pots and the wheel and not the basket, moved the damper ahead of her instruction with the bent finger leading, and did not say the back room was cold or look toward the bed. He knows the full note of a Vail pot before it leaves the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master. The promise of Corin's name to the people who burn slates stayed where it has always stayed, ungiven and not withdrawn, and *he's not Vail* stayed sealed.

**Ch 13.** He came in from the wheel-shed with slip dried to the colour of old bone up both forearms and no grey worked past the bend of his little finger. He spoke to the wheel before he spoke to anyone, told it what it had been told a hundred times --- it did not care whose hands were on it, only if they were steady --- and told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome in it and no unkindness either. He knew the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master, who would call it early and make something of it. Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low; his bent finger went in ahead of his hand at the damper and moved it, and the end of her sentence arrived at the pot instead of at him. He did not say the back room was cold and did not look toward the bed or the basket. The men who came into that room were not named and the broken birth-slates lay under the drying rack where the yard went around them and entered them into nothing; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as a lid.

**Ch 14.** On Silt 4 the room off the wheel-shed kept its finger's width of open all morning and Jorra came through with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; two men had come in since first light and spoken low, and he gave the house no name for them. The broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack where the yard went around them and entered them into nothing, and the boy kept the dusty forearms and the clean ones on one tray as two states of one morning. Jorra spoke to the wheel before he spoke to anyone — it did not care whose hands, only if they were steady — told a pot it would do, and knew the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel without saying so before a kiln-master. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low, his bent finger went in ahead of his hand at the damper and moved it, so that the end of her sentence arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence. He did not say the back room was cold, did not look toward the bed or the basket; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as a lid and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was ungiven and not withdrawn.

**Ch 15.** On Silt 14 he came through the finger's-width-open door off the wheel-shed in the afternoon with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; two men had come in since first light and spoken low and he named neither the men nor the room. The broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack where the yard went around them and entered them into nothing. He spoke to the wheel before he spoke to anyone --- it did not care whose hands, only if they were steady --- told a pot it would do, and knew the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel without saying so before a kiln-master from High Kiln. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low, his bent finger went in ahead of his hand at the damper and moved it, so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence. He did not say the back room was cold. On Silt 15 he came through again with bone-coloured slip dried to old bone up both forearms and no grey worked past the bend; the grey dust was the day before. He did not look toward the bed or into the basket. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years, and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn.

**Ch 16.** On Day 77, Silt 18, he comes through the finger's-width-open wheel-shed door with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; two men had come in since first light and spoken low in the back room and he gave the house no name for them. On Day 76 his forearms carried clean bone-coloured slip dried to old bone, and the boy kept the two states of one week on a single tray and asked neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. With Corin nothing has changed: the boy lies under the roof unwritten, *he's not Vail* is sealed as tightly as on the hour it was spoken, and the promise of his name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn. With Tarn the old equality of duty and silence holds, neither asking about the back room or the ear. **Ch 52 (bonds).** Through Ember 28–30 Jorra came through with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist had opened and not bone-coloured slip; he parted dust from slip as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, kept dusty and clean forearms on one tray and asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. Three or four men went low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on all three mornings and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold. When Sella's voice went low and unfinished at the hearth his bent finger went into the cold damper and held until the sentence reached the pot, then moved a hair's breadth and was left standing — a remembered repetition, not a change made again. The iron stayed one piece with two bites, holding and taking, with no word in Grathic for both mercies, joined to no kiln and entered in no slate. He did not look toward the bed or ask about the ear while Tarn did not ask about the room; the two silences stayed unjoined. No new scar or possession, no change of voice, no bond paid or spoken.

- **Ch 78 (bonds).** With Sella the household kept its old shape on Frost 16–18: his bent finger went into the cold damper ahead of her unfinished sentence, the end of it arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence; she asked nothing about the men or the dust. With the men unnamed in the back room he gave no name and no word that the room was cold; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name was neither given nor withdrawn. With Ilva, Hessa and the runner's saying nothing was joined: the Frost 14 saying held with no second shaping and was not laid across his dust, the damper or the boy. No bond changed hands, no secret sold and no debt paid.

- **Ch 80 (bonds).** With Sella the household kept its old shape on Frost 22, 23 and 24: his bent finger went into the cold damper ahead of her unfinished sentence, the end of it arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence; she asked nothing about the dust or the men and gave the house no name for them. With Corin nothing changed: the boy lay under the roof unwritten, *he's not Vail* was sealed and the promise of his name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. With Tarn the equality held at the place it had moved to, neither asking about the back room or the ear; the debt's stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check while his own finger stood in the damper. Men unnamed went low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on all three mornings and got no name and no word that the room was cold; he came through with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent finger and under the nails, not bone-coloured slip, kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. He spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note three turns ahead and withheld it from any kiln-master, and kept the burned slate at four, the shut-room tellings at two, the row at five askings with one shaping and no witness. The Frost 14 saying gained a third shaping from an ownerless voice on Frost 23 and was kept on a place of its own beside Hessa Rale's eighth hand with a finger between every pair, not a witness, not a cause, not a confession and not paid, and not making Hessa a reader. No bond was paid or spoken, no new scar or possession and no change of voice.

With Tarn the two silences stayed unjoined — his ear-sentence came to the calling word and stopped on all three mornings and Jorra did not ask about it, while Tarn did not ask about the back room. He parted dusty forearms from clean as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, spoke to the wheel before any person and told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note three turns ahead with air under it and a want in it and withheld the knowing, and gave the men unnamed in the back room no name and no word that the room was cold. Burned slate stood at four, the shut-room tellings at two, the three pot sayings with the Smoke 14 note apart, the row question at five askings with one shaping and no witness; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name was neither given nor withdrawn. The Frost 14 saying gained a second shaping from a voice over the boards on Frost 19 and was held beside his dust, the damper and the boy with a finger between, not joined to any of them and not making Hessa a reader. Berr's second arithmetic went up the road and was not brought back under the roof. No bond changed hands, no secret sold, no debt paid, no new scar or possession and no change of voice.

- **Ch 83 (bonds).** Sella's unfinished sentence still arrived at the pot instead of at him on all three days and the household straightened around his silence; he did not look toward the bed or the basket and did not answer with a word. He came through the finger's-width-open wheel-shed door each day with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and under the nails, not bone-coloured slip, kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray parted as clot from vessel, asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*) and told a pot *You'll do* with no welcome and no unkindness. He knew the full note that pot would give three turns before it left his hands, with air under it and a want in it, and withheld the knowing on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them, advanced and not paid. Men unnamed went low into the back room before first grey on the mornings of Days 300, 301 and 302 and got no name and no word that the room was cold; the burned slate stood at four, the shut-room tellings at two, the three pot sayings with the Smoke 14 note apart, the row at five askings with one shaping and no witness, and the Frost 14 saying at three shapings beside Hessa Rale's eighth hand with a finger between every pair, not a witness, not a cause and not paid. **Ch 85 (bonds).** Sella's unfinished sentence still arrived at the pot on all three days and the household straightened around his silence; he did not look toward the bed or answer with a word, and she asked nothing about the men, the dust or the damper. With Corin nothing changed: the boy lay under the roof unwritten, *he's not Vail* was sealed, and the promise of his name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn — the fifth telling of the burned slate gathered on the claim and he neither paid nor refused it. **New at the going of the light on Bone 8:** his bent finger stood in the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence and she let the end of it arrive at the pot; Tarn's broom checked half a beat behind the standing finger on the flagstones and went on toward the road without a word or a turn of the head. Neither man named the other's silence and the loan that can hear was not called. The boy filed it as the fourth held change on a fourth place and would not let four such changes stand up as a rule. He came through the finger's-width-open shed each day with meal-fine grey slate dust past the bent finger and under the nails, not bone-coloured slip, parted dusty forearms from clean as clot from vessel, spoke to the wheel and a pot before any person, knew the full note a pot would give three turns ahead, with air under it and a want in it, and withheld it from a High Kiln master who would have called it early; he gave the men unnamed before first grey no name and did not say the room was cold. The burned slate stood at five, the shut-room tellings at two, the pot sayings at three with the Smoke 14 note apart, the row at five askings with no witness and the Frost 14 saying at three shapings. No bond was paid or spoken, no new scar, possession or change of voice.

**Ch 84 (bonds).** Sella's unfinished sentence still arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence; he did not look toward the bed or answer with a word, and she asked nothing about the men, the dust or the damper. With Corin nothing changed: the boy lay under the roof unwritten, *he's not Vail* was sealed, and the promise of his name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn — the fifth telling of the burned slate gathered on the claim and he neither paid nor refused it. With Tarn the equality held at the place it had moved to, neither asking about the back room or the ear; his bent finger stood in the cold damper while the debt's stroke went on over the bright patch without the half-check, and Tarn's knife stayed at the edge with the unforged collector sentence beside it. The carried man's account, Ilva's strip and the warm loft stone were not brought under the wheel-shed as lessons or payments. No bond was paid or spoken, no new scar, possession or change of voice.

## Arc So Far


**Ch 117 (Days 402–404, Ilmen 8–10).** At the wheel-shed edge on Ilmen 8 and 9 and offstage on Ilmen 10, the man who is not Vail keeps his silence while the household straightens around it: dusty forearms parted from clean as clot from vessel, the full note known three turns ahead and withheld, the bent finger in the cold damper ahead of an unfinished sentence and out without a second holding. A wrong wind off High Kiln brings the sweet edge of glaze over the boards and takes it out again, a direction and not a cause. The promise stays neither given nor withdrawn, gathering interest against the burned slate and the directions toward the far-off hour when a promise may be called. Forward, not closed.
**Ch 116 (Days 399–401, Ilmen 5–7).** At the wheel-shed edge on Ilmen 5 and 6 and offstage on Ilmen 7, the man who is not Vail keeps his silence while the household straightens around it: before first grey on the sixth three or more men unnamed go low into the back room, and he comes through the finger's-width door with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust white in a fist-crease, parts dusty forearms from clean as clot from vessel, asks neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, speaks to the wheel and a pot before any person, knows the full note three turns ahead and withholds it from any High Kiln master who would call a pot early, and stands his bent finger in the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence without leaving the extra holding of Ash 26 a second time (T062/T035/T031 advanced, not paid). A man from Slate Row stands at the yard's edge that day with his hands loose; the boy files his face on its own place and joins neither the waiting nor the men unnamed to the promise under the shed, the burned slate at five or the four directions, for a doctrine is an account that has found a payer and none stood. *He's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise neither given nor withdrawn. The withheld full note gathers toward the far-off day a pot is sung before its time and something is paid for it; no new scar, wound, rank or possession and no change of voice, nothing paid, called or ended.

**Ch 115 (Days 396–398, Ilmen 2–4).** The wheel-keeper holds the shed's edge on two ordinary afternoons after the turn and gives nothing away: men unnamed go low into the back room before first grey and get no name from him and no word that the room was cold; meal-fine grey slate dust is ground small as meal past the bent little finger and white in a fist-crease, not the bone-coloured slip; dusty forearms are parted from clean as clot from vessel. He speaks to the wheel and a pot before any person, knows the full note three turns ahead and withholds it from any High Kiln master who would call it early, and puts his bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, out again without a second holding of Ash 26. *He's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn while the burned slate stands at five and the four directions gather interest against it. When word comes up of the soft-cuffed clerk set below the north bridge for an uncalled measure and of Tarn's degree toward him, the boy keeps the degree and the promise on two places with a finger between and makes no cause of them. No bond paid, sold, named or spoken; no rank, power, growth or ending. Forward, not closed.

**Ch 114 (Days 393–395, the Remembering–Ilmen 1).** The wheel-keeper holds the shed's edge through the Last Day and gives nothing away. On that afternoon men unnamed go low into the back room before first grey and get no name from him and no word that the room was cold; meal-fine grey slate dust is ground small as meal past the bent little finger and white in a fist-crease, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel, and dusty forearms are parted from clean as clot from vessel. He speaks to the wheel and a pot before any person, knows the full note three turns ahead with air under it and a want in it, and withholds it from any High Kiln master who would call it early; his bent finger goes into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence and comes out without leaving the extra holding of Ash 26 a second time. *He's not Vail* stays sealed as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years and the promise to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn while the burned slate stands at five, the shut-room tellings at two and the year turning with nothing signed. The yard's called-gone grey, the remembered names, the twelve arithmetics carried behind one unsigned figure, Tarn's ended term with the loan uncalled and the keeping's further findings bring no loosening of the promise and no word under the shed; he is offstage on Ilmen 1. No new scar, wound, rank or possession and no change of voice; the betrayal he has been lent goes on gathering interest, uncalled.

**Ch 113 (Days 390–392, Kindling–the Long Night).** The wheel-keeper holds the shed's edge through the first three of the Five and gives nothing away. On Weighing afternoon men unnamed go low into the back room before first grey and get no name from him and no word that the room was cold; meal-fine grey slate dust is ground small as meal past the bent little finger and white in a fist-crease, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel, and dusty forearms are parted from clean as clot from vessel. He speaks to the wheel and a pot before any person, knows the full note three turns ahead and withholds it from any High Kiln master who would call it early; his bent finger goes into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence and comes out without leaving the extra holding of Ash 26 a second time. *He's not Vail* stays sealed as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years and the promise to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn while the burned slate stands at five, the shut-room tellings at two and the told bell of the Long Night kept in its square beside the heard bell. The year's fire from an unnamed source, the collector's ordinary hand, the clean voice's missing name, the story's empty name, Tarn's term ending with the loan uncalled and the keeping's three further findings bring no loosening of the promise and no word under the shed. No new scar, wound, rank or possession and no change of voice; the betrayal he has been lent goes on gathering interest, uncalled.

**Ch 112 (Days 387–389, Stone 28–30).** The wheel-keeper holds the shed's edge through the year's last three days and gives nothing away. He speaks to the wheel and a pot before any person, knows the full note three turns ahead and withholds it from any High Kiln master who would call it early; his bent finger goes into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence and comes out without leaving the extra holding of Ash 26 a second time. *He's not Vail* stays sealed as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years and the promise to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn while the burned slate stands at five, the shut-room tellings at two and the four directions stand where put and do not repeat. **Ch 111.** He keeps the wheel-shed edge and the household straightens around his silence without a word from anybody through Stone 25, 26 and 27; the year's last working week adds no loosening of the promise and no name for the men in the back room. The day's meal-fine grey dust comes past the bent little finger and white in a fist-crease, dusty forearms parted from clean as clot from vessel, the wheel and a pot spoken to before any person, the full note known three turns ahead and withheld from a master who would call it early. His bent finger leads at the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence and comes out without a second holding of Ash 26; one piece of iron has two bites and no Grathic word covers both. He grows quieter when the burned-slate word goes around while nobody orders him to stop, and the promise gathers interest against the slate at five and the four directions without joining any of them. *He's not Vail* stays sealed as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years; the boy remains unwritten under the roof and the count goes on without calling either. Forward, not closed.

**Ch 78.** Through Frost 16–18 he keeps the wheel-shed edge: meal-fine grey slate dust ground past the bent finger, dusty forearms parted from clean, speech to the wheel and a pot before any person, the full note of the pot known three turns ahead with air under it and a want in it and withheld on the near-full tray. Men unnamed go low into the back room before first grey on all three mornings and get no name from him; burned slate stands at four, the shut-room tellings at two, the three pot sayings with the Smoke 14 note apart, the row question at five askings with one shaping and no witness. *He's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the boy's name is neither given nor withdrawn; the Frost 14 saying holds with no second shaping and is not joined to anything under the roof.

**Ch 72.** Through Smoke's last tend the wheel-keeper keeps the meal-fine grey dust and the two bites of the damper on trays of their own, with the burned-slate word at four, the shut-room tellings at two, the three pot sayings with the Smoke 14 note apart and the row question at five askings with one shaping and no witness. The third reading of Smoke changes the district's arithmetic and comes to nothing under this roof. He has not decided which side he is on and the men who burn slates have not decided for him; the promise of the boy's name stays ungiven as the seventh year at the debt's end presses nearer.

He enters at the wheel with his weight in the hollow his father wore and has spent three years failing to fill --- slip dried to the colour of old bone up both forearms, the left little finger bent from a splinter at eleven, a story about a fight he tells because a fight sounds more like him. In Chapter 2 he says one flat sentence about the foundling and is refused an answer, and the sentence is sealed by the boy's arrival: a child lies under the roof and nobody has written him as Vail. He does not look at his mother while obeying her instantly, speaks to the pots before people, goes quiet when the levy is spoken of, and has given none of the men who come into the back room a word they can carry away. He is the most careful keeper of the Vail fire in four generations and is quietly helping the people who want to burn every slate in Sill, and has never once thought the two facts cannot live in the same man. Through the middle tend of Grain his dust, his wheel and his withheld note kept their old order while the burned-slate word held at four and the pot sayings at three; the wrong-wind voice on Grain 15 let one saying stand without a pot lost, and the promise of the boy's name stayed ungiven (T035/T031/T065/T095 held, T061/T082).

On Days 5 and 6 the back room comes into the house on his forearms as slate dust, grey and fine, worked into the creases past the bend of the little finger, and not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel. He tells the pots *you'll do*, shifts the damper before Sella has finished telling him and shifts it again at dusk, and says *the back room's cold* to nobody in particular --- not an answer and not a question, shaped so nobody can take it further. Sella does not take it further. The boy watches the dust and wants to know which slate was broken, and whether it was a birth-slate, and keeps the questions where questions can last for years.

On Days 7, 8 and 9 the wheel keeps the house's time and the absence of dust is as much a fact as its presence was on Stone 12. He works in bone-coloured slip, speaks to the pots and the wheel and never to the basket, and moves the damper before Sella has finished: an obedience that is also a correction neither will name. He goes quieter when the levy is spoken around and quieter still at *the house's share*, and the yard straightens around him. The broken birth-slates under the drying rack and the men whose names are not spoken stay unshown; the back room is not entered in any roll the house shows, and its cold is not mentioned. He still wants the foundling outside the name and the hollow seat for himself, and has said neither.

On Days 10 through 14 the wheel keeps the house's time and the absence of dust is the fact of the five days: slip the colour of old bone on both forearms, no grey worked into the creases. He speaks to the pots and the wheel and never to the basket, says *you'll do* to a pot and that the wheel does not care whose hands are on it, and shifts the damper before Sella has finished --- an obedience that is also a correction neither will name. He goes quieter when the levy and the debt are spoken around and quieter still at the reading at the Cross. He does not say the back room was cold, does not take up Sella's silence, and does not look toward the bed where the foundling lies. The broken birth-slates under the drying rack and the men whose names are not spoken stay unshown; he still wants the boy outside the name Vail and the hollow seat for himself, and has said neither.

On Days 15 through 24 the wheel keeps the house's time and the dust comes and goes. Five clean mornings make its absence a fact; on Stone 23 it returns, grey and fine in the creases past the little finger, and the boy sets the two states of the same forearms on separate places on a tray. Jorra speaks to the pot and the wheel, never to the basket, moves the damper before Sella has finished, and goes quieter as the Last Day sweeping talk goes around. He sets a pot down on the bench and does not turn to its interval, and the boy does not turn after him. On Stone 29 a sweeping that will leave the grey where it lies until it is burned and called dealt with makes the house straighter and him quieter; on Stone 30 he has moved the damper and not looked toward the bed. The full note is in a Vail pot before it leaves the wheel, which he will not say before a kiln-master, and the boy knows he knows. The broken birth-slates under the drying rack and the men in the back room stay unshown; the foundling goes into a new year carried under the roof without a Vail name.

On Days 25 through 30 the wheel keeps the house's time and the back room is not once mentioned. The forearms carry bone-coloured slip dried to old bone and the bent little finger goes in ahead of the hand at the damper, which he moves before Sella has finished; no grey dust appears after the Last Day, and the boy, who once set the dust and the clean slip on separate places on a tray, has nothing new to file. He speaks to the pots and the wheel and never to the basket, does not look toward the bed, and goes quieter when the sweeping talk and the burned Ash come around. The full note is in a Vail pot before it leaves the wheel and he will not say so before a kiln-master; *he's not Vail* stays sealed through the turn of the year, and the foundling goes into Y.H. 813 carried under the roof unwritten.

On Days 31 through 37 the wheel keeps the house's time and the dust comes back from the room the house does not enter. On Ilmen 4 grey slate dust is worked into the creases past the bent little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; the yard goes around the space under the drying rack where whole slate should have stood and enters it. He speaks to the pots and the wheel and never to the basket, moves the damper before Sella has finished, and knows the full note of a Vail pot before it leaves the wheel but will not say so before a kiln-master, who would call it early and make something of it. *He's not Vail* stays sealed and the foundling goes on being carried under the roof unwritten.

On Days 38 through 44 the wheel keeps the house's time and the back room is not once mentioned. At dusk on Ilmen 9 he came in from the wheel-shed with bone-coloured slip dried to old bone up both forearms and went to the damper before Sella had finished telling him the fire was low, the bent finger going in ahead of his hand, and neither he nor Tarn looked toward the bed where the boy lay; they had not been told about the woman in the brook and had not asked. On Ilmen 11 the forearms were clean of slate dust and the bent finger led again at the damper; he told a pot *You'll do*, told the wheel it did not care whose hands were on it, only if they were steady, said nothing about the back room and did not look toward the basket. He agreed with nobody about the thaw and contradicted nobody, and the yard straightened around his silence without an order. Through the Fish Blessing at the Weir he stayed in the wheel-shed, and *he's not Vail* stayed sealed for all seven days.

On Days 45 through 47 the wheel keeps the house's time and the back room is not once mentioned. The forearms carry bone-coloured slip dried to old bone and no slate dust past the bend of the little finger; the bent finger leads at the damper ahead of Sella's sentence and the end of her instruction goes to the pot. He speaks to the pots and the wheel and never to the bed, tells a pot *You'll do*, and says nothing about the back room. He knows the full note of a Vail pot before it leaves the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master, and the boy keeps the knowing where questions can last for years. Garl's proverbs go to the pot and the fire and never to the basket, and the clean dark line is not swept. *He's not Vail* stays sealed through three more days and the foundling goes on being carried under the roof unwritten.

On Days 48 through 54 the wheel keeps the house's time and the dust comes back from the room the house does not enter. On Day 50 he comes in from the back room with fine grey slate dust worked into the creases past the bend of his little finger and says nothing about where he has been; the men are not named and the broken birth-slates stay under the drying rack, gone around and entered by nobody. On the other mornings the forearms carry bone-coloured slip and the boy keeps the two states apart. He tells a pot it will do and the wheel it does not care whose hands, only if they are steady, and knows the full note of a Vail pot before it leaves the wheel but would not say so before a kiln-master, who would call it early and make something of it. The bent finger goes in ahead of his hand at the damper ahead of Sella's instruction; he does not say the back room was cold and does not look toward the bed or the basket. *He's not Vail* stays sealed through all seven days.

On Days 55 through 60 the wheel keeps the house's time and the back room is not once mentioned. The forearms carry bone-coloured slip dried to old bone and no slate dust worked past the bend of the little finger, and the boy keeps the clean state apart from the dust days without being able to ask what either means. He speaks to the wheel and the pots and never to the basket or the bed, tells the wheel it does not care whose hands are on it, only if they are steady, and knows the full note of a Vail pot before it leaves the wheel but will not say so before a kiln-master, who would call it early and make something of it. His bent finger goes in ahead of his hand at the damper ahead of Sella's instruction so that the end of her sentence arrives at the pot instead of at him; the yard straightens around his silence without an order. He does not say the back room was cold, the men in that room are not named, and the broken birth-slates stay under the drying rack, gone around and entered by nobody. The promise of Corin's name to the people who burn slates stays where it has always stayed, ungiven and not withdrawn, and *he's not Vail* stays sealed through the turning of the month and the coming of Silt.

On Days 61 through 67 the wheel keeps the house's time and the room the house does not enter puts its dust into the yard again. On Silt 4 Jorra comes through the finger's-width-open door with grey slate dust past the bent little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel, says nothing about where he has been, and lets the men in that room stay unnamed. He keeps the two states of his forearms as one man's two mornings and the boy keeps them on the same tray without asking which slate was broken or whether it was a birth-slate. Broken birth-slates stay buried under the lowest rack and the space above them is gone around as wide as a pail of hot water set where a child might knock it, entered by nobody. He speaks to the wheel and the pots and never to the basket or the bed, knows the full note of a Vail pot before it leaves the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master who would call it early and make something of it, and moves the damper with the bent finger leading ahead of Sella's sentence so that the end of her instruction arrives at the pot. The yard straightens around his silence without an order. He does not say the back room is cold and does not look toward the bed; *he's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the foundling's name stays ungiven and not withdrawn, while the hollow seat waits for his weight and the foundling goes on being carried under the roof unwritten.

On Days 68 through 74 the wheel keeps the house's time through the flood and the room the house does not enter puts its dust into the yard on Silt 14. Jorra comes through the finger's-width-open door with grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of the little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel, says nothing about the two men who came in since first light and gives them no name; the next afternoon he comes through with clean bone-coloured slip and no grey past the bend. The broken birth-slates stay under the lowest rack and the space above them is gone around as wide as a pail of hot water set where a child might knock it, entered by nobody. He speaks to the wheel and the pots and never to the basket or the bed, knows the full note of a Vail pot before it leaves the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master who would call it early and make something of it, and moves the damper with the bent finger leading ahead of Sella's unfinished instruction so that the end of her sentence arrives at the pot. The yard straightens around his silence without an order. He does not say the back room is cold and does not look toward the bed; *he's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates stays ungiven and not withdrawn, while the hollow seat waits for his weight and the foundling goes on being carried under the roof unwritten.

On Days 75 through 77 the wheel keeps the house's time through the mud the flood left, and the room the house does not enter puts its dust into the yard once more. Jorra comes through on Silt 18 with grey slate dust past the bent finger and clean bone-coloured forearms the day before, keeps the two states as one man's two mornings, and gives the men in the back room no name. The bent finger leads at the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence and the household straightens around his silence; the full note is in a Vail pot before it leaves the wheel and he will not say so before a kiln-master. The broken birth-slates stay buried under the lowest rack and the space above them is gone around as wide as a pail of hot water set where a child might knock it, entered by nobody. He does not say the back room is cold and does not look toward the bed; *he's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the name stays ungiven and not withdrawn, while the hollow seat waits for his weight and the foundling goes on being carried under the roof unwritten.

On Days 78 through 84 the wheel keeps the house's time through a week in which nothing is settled, and the room the house does not enter puts its dust into the yard once more. On Silt 22 he comes through the finger's-width-open door with grey slate dust past the bent little finger, not bone-coloured slip, and gives no name to the men who came in before light; the broken birth-slates stay under the lowest rack and the yard goes around the space above them as wide as a pail of hot water set where a child might knock it. He keeps dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray as two states of one man's mornings and asks neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. He speaks to the wheel before anybody and to the pots and never to the basket, knows the full note before it leaves the wheel and will not say it before a kiln-master, and moves the damper with the bent finger leading ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so that the household straightens around his silence. He does not say the back room is cold and does not look toward the bed; *he's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the name stays ungiven and not withdrawn, while the hollow seat waits for his weight and the foundling goes on being carried under the roof unwritten.

On Days 85 through 90 the wheel keeps the house's time across the turning of Silt into Seed and the room the house does not enter puts its dust into the yard again. On Silt 27 he comes through the finger's-width-open wheel-shed door with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; men came in since first light and spoke low in the back room and he gives the house no name for them. The broken birth-slates stay under the lowest rack, gone around by the yard as wide as a pail of hot water set where a child might knock it and entered by nobody. He keeps dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray as two states of one man's mornings and asks neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. He speaks to the wheel before anyone --- *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* --- tells a pot *You'll do*, and knows the full note of a Vail pot before it leaves the wheel and will not say so before a kiln-master from High Kiln. When Sella has not finished telling him the fire is low, his bent finger goes into the damper ahead of her sentence and moves it, cold iron taking the cold out of his fingertip, so the end of her instruction arrives at the pot and the household straightens around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted; he has not answered her with a word and has not looked at her, which is how he obeys her instantly. He reads the damper as any iron that stops a thing from running: the same bite that keeps a vessel whole can take the hand off a man, and no word in Grathic holds both mercies in one. On Seed 1 he is heard at the wheel and not seen, a low note and the word to a pot, with no figure coming out of the shed onto the yard. He does not say the back room is cold and does not look toward the bed or the basket. *He's not Vail* stays sealed as tightly as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years, and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn while the hollow seat waits for his weight.

On Days 91 through 93 the wheel keeps the house's time and the room the house does not enter puts its dust into the yard once more. On Seed 3 he comes through the finger's-width-open wheel-shed door with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; men came in since first light and spoke low in the back room and he gives the house no name for them. The broken birth-slates stay under the lowest rack, gone around as wide as a pail of hot water set where a child might knock it and entered by nobody, and he keeps dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray as two states of one man's mornings without asking which slate was broken or whether it was a birth-slate. He speaks to the wheel before anyone --- it does not care whose hands, only if they are steady --- tells a pot *You'll do*, knows the full note of a Vail pot before it leaves the wheel and will not say so before a kiln-master from High Kiln. When Sella has not finished telling him the fire is low, his bent finger goes into the damper ahead of her sentence, the cold iron taking the cold out of his fingertip, and the end of her instruction arrives at the pot while the household straightens around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted. He reads the damper as an iron gate and a bite that holds warmth in and lets it out by degrees, the same bite that keeps a vessel whole and can take the hand off a man, and no word in Grathic holds both mercies; he keeps the reading to himself, an anatomy of the house's danger exact and worth nothing. He does not say the back room is cold and does not look toward the bed or the basket. *He's not Vail* stays sealed as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years, the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn, and the hollow seat waits for his weight.

He reads the iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and can take the hand off a man, with no Grathic word for both mercies, and files it as an object joined to no kiln, exact and worth nothing. On Days 116 through 119 the wheel keeps the house's time through the end of the planting and the room the house does not enter puts its dust into the yard once more. On Seed 28, the only day he is on stage, men came up the Clay Yard before first grey with their collars high and spoke low in the finger's-width-open back room; he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, and came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel. This had been worked inward, into the creases, the way grit works into a knee; he kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. The broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, gone around as wide as a pail of hot water set where a child might knock it and entered by nobody. He spoke to the wheel before anyone — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — told a pot *You'll do*, knew the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master come up from High Kiln who would call it early and make something of it. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of her sentence, the iron taking the cold out of his fingertip and giving a little back in reverse of the core, so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted. He read the iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and the bite that can take the hand off a man, with no Grathic word for both mercies, and filed it as an object worth nothing, joined to no firing. Tarn's stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check while the stones kept the place where it had stopped; neither man named the other's silence. He did not look toward the bed or into the basket. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as the hour it was spoken three years ago, and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. No new scar or possession, no change of voice.

On Days 120 through 122 the wheel keeps the house's time and the room the house does not enter puts its dust into the yard again. On Reed 2, the only day he is on stage, men came up the Clay Yard before first grey with their collars high and went low-voiced into the finger's-width-open room off the wheel-shed; he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, and came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel. He kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray without asking which slate had been broken or whether it was a birth-slate, and the broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, gone around and entered by nobody. He spoke to the wheel before anyone — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — told a pot *You'll do*, knew the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master from High Kiln. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low his bent finger went into the cold damper ahead of her sentence, the iron taking the cold out of his fingertip, so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted; he did not answer with a word and did not look at her. He read the iron as the bite that holds and the bite that can take the hand off a man, one piece of iron and two mercies with no Grathic word for both, and filed it as an object worth nothing, joined to no kiln. The debt's stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check, the stones keeping the shallow bright patch where it had stopped, and neither man named the other's silence. He did not look toward the bed or the basket; *he's not Vail* stays sealed as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years, the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn, and the hollow seat waits for his weight while the foundling goes on being carried under the roof unwritten.

On Days 123 through 125 the wheel keeps the house's time and the room the house does not enter puts its dust into the yard once more. On Reed 5 men unnamed came up the Clay Yard before first grey with their collars high and spoke low in the finger's-width-open back room; Jorra gave the house no name for them, did not say the room was cold, and came through with fine grey slate dust worked small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger, keeping dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray without asking which slate was broken or whether it was a birth-slate. The broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, gone around as wide as a pail of hot water set where a child might knock it and entered by nobody. He spoke to the wheel and the pots and never to the basket, knew the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel and would not say it before a kiln-master from High Kiln who would call it early and make something of it, and let the kiln-master go with nothing a slate could carry. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low, his bent finger went into the cold damper ahead of her sentence, the iron taking the cold out of his fingertip and moving the tongue a hair's breadth; the end of her instruction arrived at the pot, the household straightened around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted, and he neither answered nor looked at her. He read the iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and can take the hand off a man, with no Grathic word for both mercies, and set the reading beside the two tellings of the shut counting room with no third telling and no joining. Behind his standing finger Tarn's stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check, the stones keeping the shallow bright patch where it had stopped, and neither man named the other's silence. *He's not Vail* stays sealed as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years, and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn.

On Days 126 through 128 the wheel goes on and the back room stays shut. He is offstage on Reed 7, 8 and 9 except for the one morning of Day 127, and on that morning men unnamed come up the Clay Yard before first grey with their collars high and speak low in the room off the wheel-shed; the door stands a finger's width open and is not entered, and he gives the house no name for them and does not say the room was cold. He comes through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bend of the little finger — not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel — keeps dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray, and asks neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate; the broken birth-slates lie under the lowest rack and nobody enters them. He speaks to the wheel before anyone, *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*, tells a pot *You'll do* and knows its full note before it leaves the wheel; when a kiln-master come up from High Kiln would call it early and make something of it he withholds the knowing and says nothing a slate could carry. When Sella has not finished telling him the fire is low his bent finger goes into the cold iron of the damper ahead of her sentence, moves the tongue a hair's breadth and leaves it standing, and the household straightens around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted; the boy reads the iron once more as the bite that holds a vessel whole and the bite that can take the hand off a man, with no word in Grathic for both mercies, and files it as an object worth nothing joined to no kiln. Tarn's stroke goes on over the flagstones without the half-check while the stones keep the shallow bright patch where it stopped; neither man names the other's silence, and when the ear's sentence comes a second time to the threshold of the word that night he does not ask about it. *He's not Vail* stays sealed, the promise of the foundling's name to the slate-burners is neither given nor withdrawn, and the two tellings of the shut counting room stay beside the iron with no third. Nothing is named, opened, joined or paid.

The men in the room off the shed remain three or four shapes with no names, and the dust ground small as meal past the bent finger still waits for the question he will not ask. The damper holds mercy and mutilation in one bite with no Grathic word for both; the promise of the foundling's name to the slate-burners is neither given nor withdrawn. The equality under the roof holds at its moved place, Tarn's stroke going on without the half-check and neither man naming the other's silence.

On Days 135 through 137 the wheel keeps the house's time and the room the house does not enter puts its dust into the yard once more.
