The broken birth-slates stay under the rack and nobody enters them; the two tellings of the shut counting room stay beside the iron with no third. He does not ask about the ear and Tarn does not ask about the room, and *he's not Vail* stays sealed.

On Days 138 through 140 the household keeps the equality it moved to by fractions and the broken slates stay unentered. On Day 140, before first grey, three or four men with their collars high go low into the finger's-width-open back room; he gives them no name and does not say the room was cold. He comes through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger and under the nails, keeps dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and asks neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. He speaks to the wheel before anyone --- *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* --- tells a pot *You'll do*, knows its full note before it leaves the wheel and withholds it from a High Kiln kiln-master who says the pot is singing before its time and will sing itself out early; the knowing goes on the tray of near-full answers, advanced and not paid. His bent finger goes into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, moves the tongue a hair's breadth and leaves it standing, and the boy reads the iron once more as mercy and mutilation in one bite with no Grathic word for both, filed joined to no kiln. At the hearth he does not ask about the ear and Tarn does not ask about the room; the loan that can hear stays uncalled and Tarn's sixth stopping draws a breath that is not spent. The two tellings of the shut counting room stand beside the damper with no third, and the men in the back room are not joined to them. Nothing is said of the foundling's name, no slate is burned in his hearing, and the promise stays where it was put.

Through Reed 22 and 23 he keeps the room and the wheel between himself and the boy. The fine grey dust comes out of the back room ground small as meal past the bent finger on the morning the unnamed men go in before first grey, and he keeps dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray, asks which slate was broken of nobody and whether it was a birth-slate of nobody, and gives the men no name; the broken birth-slates stay under the lowest rack and the room stands a finger's width open. He speaks to the wheel before anyone, tells a pot it will do, knows its full note three turns ahead and withholds the knowing from a High Kiln kiln-master who would call it singing before its time, and puts his bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightens around his silence — the boy reading one iron with two bites, mercy and mutilation in one Grathic-unnameable hold, filed as an object joined to no kiln. He does not look toward the bed and does not ask about the ear; *he's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn. He is offstage on Day 143 and nothing in him has changed hands.

**Ch 39.** Day 161 is his day under the wheel-shed and at the cold damper (offstage on Days 160 and 162). The household straightened around a sentence that had been allowed to finish and nobody said a word of the waiting; the boy filed it on a place of its own as a change held and unspoken, a movement of the existing equality and not a reconciliation, a hearing or a payment, with no payer named and no slate entered, kept apart from Tarn’s unchecking stroke and the nine stoppings (T062/T035/T031, T061/T082). He did not look toward the basket, did not ask about the ear, and gave no word about the waiting; the two silences stayed unjoined and the loan stayed uncalled (T036/T076). He came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist opened, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and asked neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, and the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack were entered by nobody (T009/T013/T092). He spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note three turns before it left the wheel and withheld the knowing from a High Kiln kiln-master come up on a wrong wind with the sweetish lead edge at one mouth-corner, who said a pot singing before its time generally sings itself out early; the knowing went to the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them, advanced and not paid, and no pot was lost (T061/T082). **The chapter's one meaningful change, paid small and non-fatally and seen from the yard's edge:** on Day 188 the sweetish edge of lead glaze rode the wrong wind off High Kiln, a rack-worker set a pot down wrong and the pot gave its high thin note before its time and then sang itself out early with the woman going on with her hands and making no lesson of it, and two men carried a glaze-room hand out of the kiln and across the yard toward the clay road, alive and breathing, sweet-sickness on his breath and the pale line at his gum, one carrier's thumb hooked under his arm to keep the head from lopping. The High Kiln saying was not made true as a lesson; the pot was swept around and the cost of the carried man stayed entered in no slate (T077 advanced, not paid). When Sella's instruction went low and unfinished at the hearth on all three days his bent finger went to the cold iron of the damper and waited until the sentence reached the pot, then moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing; the fraction was held and unspoken, a remembered holding on the second and third days and not a new change, not paired with the Day 166 beat beneath the loaf shelf or the Day 171 flagstone half-check into a rule with a payer (T062). While his finger stood in the iron Tarn's stroke went on over the flagstones past the shallow bright patch without the half-check, and neither man named the other's silence (T036/T076). He did not look toward the bed or the basket, did not ask about the ear and gave the men in the back room no name; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years, and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn.

On Days 189 through 191 he comes through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent finger, dusty and clean forearms kept on one tray, asks neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, and gives the men who went low into the back room before first grey on Days 190 and 191 no name. He speaks to the wheel before anyone and tells a pot *You'll do*, knows its full note three turns ahead and withholds the knowing from a man on the wrong wind who would make a lesson of an early note; the knowing goes to the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them, advanced and not paid, and no pot is lost. His bent finger stands in the cold damper until Sella's unfinished sentence reaches the pot on all three days, a remembered holding and not a new change, and the two shut-room tellings stay two with no third. *He's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates stays ungiven. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar or possession.

Through Days 192 through 194, Ember 13–15, Thirdday to Fifthday, the wheel keeps the house's time and the room the house does not enter puts its dust into the yard on all three mornings. Three or four men with their collars high went low before first grey into the finger's-width-open back room off the wheel-shed and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, and the two shut-room tellings stayed two with no third (T035/T031, T065/T095). He came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist had opened, not bone-coloured slip; he kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and asked neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, and the broken birth-slates beneath the lowest rack were entered by nobody (T009/T013/T092). He spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome in it and no unkindness either, knew the full note that pot would give three turns before it left his hands and withheld the knowing from a man come up on a wrong wind who said a pot singing before its time generally sings itself out early; the man took his silence for agreement and went off up the yard with his saying, the knowing went to the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them, advanced and not paid, and no pot was lost (T061/T082). The sweet edge of lead glaze came off High Kiln on a wrong wind and was taken as a direction, not a name and not a cause, and went out on the same wind; the pot that had sung itself out early stood swept around and was not made an omen or a lesson so soon after the carrying-out, and nobody named what ailed the man carried out on Day 188 (T077, T064 held). When Sella's instruction went low and unfinished at the hearth on all three days his bent finger went to the cold iron of the damper and waited until the sentence reached the pot, then moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing — a remembered repetition, not a change made again, not paired with the Day 166 beat beneath the shelf or the Day 171 flagstone half-check into a rule with a payer (T062). The damper stayed one piece of iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no word in Grathic that would carry both mercies in one mouthful, joined to no kiln and entered in no slate (T062/T035). While his finger stood in the iron Tarn's stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check and neither man named the other's silence (T036/T076). He did not look toward the bed or the basket and did not ask about the ear; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years, and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates stayed ungiven and not withdrawn. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar or possession, no change of voice.

Through Days 195–197 (Ember 16–18) the wheel keeps the house's time and the room the house does not enter puts its dust into the yard on two mornings. Three or four men unnamed went low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on Days 196 and 197 and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold; the two shut-room tellings stay two with no third. He came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist opened, not bone-coloured slip, kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and asked neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. He spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note three turns ahead and withheld it from a man come up on a wrong wind whose saying about a pot singing early stayed a saying about a pot, with no pot lost; the sweet edge of lead glaze rode the wrong wind and went out on the same wind, taken as a direction and not a second edge paid so soon after the carrying-out. When Sella's instruction went low at the hearth his bent finger went into the cold damper and waited until the sentence reached the pot, then moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing — a remembered repetition and not a new change, not paired with the Day 161 fraction, the Day 166 beat or the Day 171 half-check into a rule. *He's not Vail* stays sealed as tightly as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years, and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn.

Through Days 198–203 (Ember 19–24) the wheel keeps the house's time and the room the house does not enter puts its dust into the yard on the last working days of the kiln month. When Sella's voice went low and unfinished his bent finger went into the cold damper ahead of her hand and held until the sentence reached the pot, then moved the tongue a hair's breadth and was left standing — a remembered repetition and not a new change, not stood up with the Day 161 fraction, the Day 166 beat or the Day 171 half-check into a rule. Through Days 204–206 (Ember 25–27) the wheel keeps the house's time and the room the house does not enter puts its dust into the yard through the kiln month's last working week. A word comes up from Slate Row, spoken over the boards and not to the basket, that a slate was burned in the night; nobody names whose, or why, and he grows quieter whenever it goes around the boards, giving the house no name for anything. He speaks to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), tells a pot *You'll do*, knows its full note three turns ahead and withholds the knowing from a man come up on a wrong wind whose saying about a pot singing early stays a saying about a pot, with no pot lost; the knowing goes to the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them, advanced and not paid. When Sella's voice goes low and unfinished his bent finger goes into the cold damper ahead of her hand and holds until the sentence reaches the pot, then moves a hair's breadth and is left standing — a remembered repetition and not a change made again, not paired with the Day 161 fraction, the Day 166 beat or the Day 171 half-check into a rule. The iron stays one piece with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no word in Grathic for both mercies, joined to no kiln and entered in no slate. He does not ask about the ear while Tarn does not ask about the room; *he's not Vail* stays sealed as tightly as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years, and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn. Forward, not closed.

**Ch 52.** The kiln month goes out without a boundary drawn and the arc's great subject becomes a second telling of the burned-slate direction that gathers the Broken Slates' claim without paying it. Days 207, 208 and 209 are lived one dated day at a time; Jorra comes through with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent finger and under the nails, parts dust from slip as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, keeps dusty and clean forearms on one tray and asks neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. Three or four unnamed men go low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey and he gives the house no name for them and does not say the room is cold; the two shut-room tellings stay two with no third. The word from Slate Row goes around the boards a second time — a slate burned in the night, no owner, room, hand at the flame or reason — and he grows quieter and keeps it on a place of its own beside the dust and the two tellings with a hand between them, a direction arriving nowhere, not a cause and not something a clerk could chart. He speaks to the wheel before anyone, tells a pot *You'll do*, knows its full note three turns ahead and withholds it from a man on a wrong wind whose saying stays a saying and costs no pot. When Sella's sentence goes low and unfinished at the hearth his bent finger goes into the cold damper and holds until the sentence reaches the pot, then moves a hair's breadth and is left standing: a remembered repetition and not a change made again, not stood up with the Day 161 fraction, the Day 166 beat beneath the shelf or the Day 171 flagstone half-check into a rule. The iron stays one piece with two bites, holding and taking, with no Grathic word for both mercies, joined to no kiln and entered in no slate. *He's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates stays ungiven and not withdrawn. Forward, not closed.

**Ch 53.** Ember gives way to Grain without a boundary drawn and the arc's great subject becomes the month turning with no mark at the turning. Days 210, 211 and 212 are lived one dated day at a time; Jorra comes through with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent finger and under the nails, parts dust from slip as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, keeps dusty and clean forearms on one tray and asks neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. He grows quieter when the burned-slate word goes around the boards and keeps it on a place of its own beside the dust and the two tellings with a hand between the three, a direction arriving nowhere with no owner, room, hand at the flame or reason, not a cause and not something a clerk could chart. He speaks to the wheel before anyone, tells a pot *You'll do*, knows its full note three turns ahead and withholds it from a man on a wrong wind whose saying stays a saying and costs no pot. When Sella's sentence goes low and unfinished at the hearth his bent finger goes into the cold damper and holds until the sentence reaches the pot, then moves a hair's breadth and is left standing: a remembered repetition and not a change made again, not stood up with the Day 161 fraction, the Day 166 beat beneath the shelf or the Day 171 flagstone half-check into a rule. The iron stays one piece with two bites, holding and taking, with no Grathic word for both mercies, joined to no kiln and entered in no slate. *He's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates stays ungiven and not withdrawn. Forward, not closed.

**Ch 54.** Through Days 213–215, Grain 4–6, the wheel and the back room keep their old shape and the two wrong-wind sayings stand as sayings about a pot. Three or more men unnamed go low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on all three mornings and he gives the house no name for them and does not say the room was cold; the two shut-room tellings stay two with no third (T035/T031, T065/T095). He comes through with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist has opened, not bone-coloured slip; he parts dusty forearms from clean ones as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, keeps them on one tray and asks neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, while the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack stay entered by nobody (T009/T013/T092). **The sayings:** a kiln-hand come up from High Kiln on a wrong wind says the pot that sang before its time last month has not been heard from since; the boy takes the change apart as a direction with no hand, owner, room, cause or figure, and Jorra, who knew that pot's full note three turns before it left his hands and knows the nearly-full answer with air under it and a want in it, lets the saying stand. He gives no name to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188, makes no lesson of the green on the wind, and no pot is lost on the fifth morning of Grain (T061/T082 advanced and unpaid, T064, T035/T031, T077). On Day 215 the burned-slate word comes over the boards a third time from Slate Row, spoken on the same breath as the barley price and the weather, with no owner, room, hand at the flame or reason; he grows quieter whenever it goes around and the household straightens around the quiet without an order, the quiet going before him into the shed the way cold air goes before a door, and the word stays a direction arrived nowhere and not a third telling (T035/T031/T065/T095 advanced, not paid). He speaks to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), tells a pot *You'll do*, and when Sella's voice goes low his bent finger goes into the cold damper and holds until the sentence reaches the pot, then moves a hair's breadth and is left standing — a remembered repetition, not a change made again, not stood up with the three held changes into a rule (T062). *He's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the boy's name is neither given nor withdrawn; the broken birth-slates stay entered by nobody and the two silences stay unjoined. Forward, not closed.

**Ch 55.** Through Days 216–218, Grain 7–9, the wheel and the back room keep their old shape while the claim of the Broken Slates gathers interest and is not paid. Three or more men unnamed go low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey and get no name; the two shut-room tellings stay two with no third, and the burned-slate word comes over the boards a fourth time from Slate Row, a direction arrived nowhere and not a cause. He comes through with meal-fine grey slate dust parted from slip as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, keeps dusty and clean forearms on one tray and asks neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. A wrong wind brings a third shaping of the pot saying — gone quiet on every row come the thaw — and he lets it stand as a saying, knowing the pot's full note three turns ahead and withholding it, with no pot lost and no name for what ailed the man carried out on Day 188. He speaks to the wheel before anyone, tells a pot *You'll do*, and holds the cold damper until Sella's sentence reaches the pot as a remembered repetition, not a change made again. His quiet goes before him into the shed the way a cold air goes before a door, the household straightening around it without an order; *he's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the boy's name is neither given nor withdrawn. Forward, not closed.

Through Grain 10–12 the wheel and the back room keep their old shape. He is offstage at the market where the seventh colour and seventh shape are gathered, and at the wheel-shed on the two tending days his meal-fine grey dust goes past the bent finger while men unnamed go low into the finger's-width-open room before first grey and get no name, the two shut-room tellings still two. The three shapings of the pot saying and the fourth burned-slate telling stay directions arrived nowhere; he knows his pot's note and withholds it, tells the wheel and the pot their old sentences, and lets his finger wait at the cold damper until Sella's sentence reaches the pot as a remembered repetition. *He's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the boy's name stays ungiven.

Through Grain 13–18 the wheel and the back room keep their old shape while the claim of the Broken Slates gathers interest and is not paid. Three or more men unnamed go low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on all three mornings of the dressing and get no name; the two shut-room tellings stay two with no third, and the burned-slate word holds at four tellings out of Slate Row with no fifth, a direction arrived nowhere and joined to no cause. He comes through with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent finger and under the nails, parts dust from slip as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, keeps dusty and clean forearms on one tray and asks neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. He speaks to the wheel before anyone, tells a pot *You'll do*, knows its full note three turns ahead and holds the near-full answer with air under it and a want in it back on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them. Three shapings of the pot saying stand as sayings about a pot: on a wrong wind one goes over the boards with no owner, room, hand at the flame, figure or reason, and the sweet edge of lead glaze touches the fold and goes out on the wind that brought it, a direction and not a second edge paid; no pot is lost, no fourth likeness stands up as a cause and no name is given to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188. When Sella's sentence goes low his bent finger goes into the cold damper and holds until the sentence reaches the pot, then moves a hair's breadth and is left standing: a remembered repetition and not a change made again, not paired with the Day 161 fraction, the Day 166 beat beneath the shelf or the Day 171 flagstone half-check. His quiet goes before him into the shed and the household straightens around it without an order. The first grain is dressed and stored under wool without a figure and the returning-line place is gone around with a look and no number; *he's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the boy's name ungiven. Forward into Grain 19, not closed.

Through Grain 19–21 the burned-slate word holds at four tellings out of Slate Row, a direction arrived nowhere and still not a third telling of the counting room shut in the war, and the three shapings of the pot saying stand as sayings with no fourth likeness made a cause; the Grain 21 question about a row is a different instrument with no owner yet, filed on its own seventh place with a finger between it and the sayings, the slate, the shut room, the hearings and the count. Men unnamed go low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey and get no name from him, and the meal-fine grey dust goes past the bent finger and under the nails while clean forearms stay on the other tray. He knows the full note of a Vail pot three turns before it leaves his hands — with air under it and a want in it — and withholds it from men on wrong winds whose sayings stay sayings, with no pot lost and no name given to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188. His bent finger stands in the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence as a remembered repetition, one iron with two bites — the bite that holds a vessel whole and the bite that can take the hand off a man — with no Grathic word for both mercies and no rule stood up out of three held changes. He grows quieter whenever the burned-slate word goes around and the household straightens around the quiet without an order. He is the most careful keeper of the Vail fire in four generations and is quietly helping the people who want to burn every slate in Sill, and has never once thought these two facts cannot live in the same man. *He's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the foundling's name stays ungiven.

Through Smoke 1–3 (Days 240–242) the wheel and the back room keep their old shape under a roof in which the first firing of the month has come and gone without a lesson. Three or more men unnamed go low into the finger's-width-open room before first grey on all three mornings and get no name from him and no third telling; he comes through with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist has opened, parts dust from slip as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, keeps dusty and clean forearms on one tray and asks neither which slate broke nor whether it was a birth-slate. He speaks to the wheel first, tells a pot *You'll do*, knows its full note three turns ahead with air under it and a want in it and withholds the knowing on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them. The three shapings of the pot saying stand as sayings; on Smoke 3 the sweet edge of lead glaze rides a wrong wind off High Kiln over the boards, touches the fold and goes out on it, and he lets one saying stand again without making a lesson of the green, losing a pot or naming what ailed the man carried out on Day 188. The burned-slate word holds at four from Slate Row, still not a third telling of the shut room; the row question holds four askings with no owner, room, hand at the kiln, cause or witness, and he does not claim it or supply one. When Sella's sentence goes low his bent finger goes into the cold damper ahead of it, holds until the end arrives at the pot, moves a hair's breadth and is left standing — a remembered repetition paired with no other fraction, one piece of iron with two bites and no word in Grathic for both. *He's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the boy's name stays ungiven. No new scar or possession, no bond paid or spoken.

Through Smoke 4–6 (Days 243–245) the wheel and the back room keep their old shape under a roof whose first firing is behind and not looked at again. No new scar or possession, no bond paid or spoken.

Through Smoke 10–12 (Days 249–251) he is at the wheel-shed edge, the cold damper, the kiln mouth, the drying racks and the Clay Yard on all three days. He comes through with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist had opened, not bone-coloured slip; parts dust from slip as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, keeps dusty and clean forearms on one tray and asks neither which slate broke nor whether it was a birth-slate, while the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack are entered by nobody. Three or more men unnamed go low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on all three mornings and get no name from him, and he does not say the room was cold; the two tellings of the room shut in the war stay two with no third. He speaks to the wheel before he speaks to any person (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), tells a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness, knows its full note three turns before it leaves his hands with air under it and a want in it, and withholds the knowing on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them, advanced and not paid. The three shapings of the pot saying stay sayings with no fourth likeness made a cause; the burned-slate word holds at four tellings out of Slate Row with no fifth and still not a third telling of the shut room; the row question stays at five askings with no owner, witness, cause or row named. When Sella's sentence goes low his bent finger goes into the cold damper ahead of it, takes the cold out, holds until the end of it arrives at the pot, moves a hair's breadth and is left standing — a remembered repetition paired with no other fraction, one piece of iron with two bites and no Grathic word for both mercies, joined to no kiln. Smoke's first reading goes up the clay road in Berr's locked box and changes the district's arithmetic where two hundred people stand, and neither the reading nor the house comes to meet the other; he gives no name to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188 and no lesson to the green. *He's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn. No new scar or possession, no change of voice.

Through Smoke 13–15 (Days 252–254) the wheel and the back room keep their old shape under a roof whose first firing is behind and not looked at again. Men unnamed go low before first grey on all three mornings and get no name; the two shut-room tellings stay two and the burned-slate word stays at four, a direction arrived nowhere. He comes through with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger, white in the creases where a fist had opened, not bone-coloured slip; parts dust from slip as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, keeps dusty and clean forearms on one tray, speaks to the wheel before he speaks to anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), tells a pot *You'll do*, knows its full note three turns ahead with air under it and a want in it, and withholds the knowing on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them. The three shapings of the pot saying stay sayings with no fourth likeness made a cause; on Smoke 14 a pot set down wrong at the racks gives its high thin note before its time and the woman sweeps around it and says something practical about the slip, so no pot is lost, no lesson is made and no name is given to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188. The sweet edge of lead glaze rides a wrong wind off High Kiln over the boards and goes out on it without becoming a cause. When Sella's sentence goes low his bent finger goes into the cold damper ahead of it, takes the cold out, holds until the end arrives at the pot, moves a hair's breadth and is left standing — one piece of iron with two bites and no Grathic word for both, joined to no kiln. The row question is not asked a sixth time and stands at five askings with no witness and no row named. *He's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the boy's name stays ungiven. No new scar or possession, no bond paid or spoken.

Through Smoke 16–18 the wheel and the back room keep their old shape. He comes through each morning with meal-fine grey slate dust past the bent finger and white in the creases of a fist, parts dusty forearms from clean ones on one tray, gives the men who went into the room no name and does not say the room was cold, and keeps the two tellings of the shut room at two. He speaks to the wheel before anyone, tells a pot it will do, knows its full note three turns ahead and withholds it; the three shapings of the pot saying stand with the premature note from Smoke 14 on its own place and no fourth likeness made a cause, the burned-slate word stands at four and the row question at five askings with no witness. When Sella's sentence goes low his bent finger goes into the cold damper ahead of the end of it, takes the cold out, holds and is left standing — a remembered repetition joined to no kiln. He does not ask about the ear while Tarn does not ask about the room; *he's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn.

Through Smoke 19–21 he holds the old shape at the wheel and the damper and gives nothing up. Men unnamed go low into the back room before first grey on the 19th and 21st and get no name from him and no word that the room was cold; meal-fine grey slate dust is worked past the bent little finger, white in the creases of a fist, parted from clean slip; he speaks to the wheel before any person and tells a pot *You'll do*, knows its full note three turns ahead with air under it and a want in it and withholds the knowing on the near-full tray. Three pot sayings stay three with the premature note from Smoke 14 on its own place and no fourth likeness made a cause; the burned-slate word from Slate Row holds at four and the shut-room tellings at two; the ownerless row question stands at five askings with no witness and no row named (T035/T031/T062, T065/T095, T101). Sella's unfinished sentence still finishes at the pot, *he's not Vail* is sealed and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn; the equality with Tarn holds at the place it moved to, neither asking about the back room or the ear. Smoke's second arithmetic goes up the road in Berr's locked box on the 20th and makes no change under the wheel-shed (T015/T041/T047).

Through Smoke 22–24 he holds the old shape at the wheel and the damper and gives nothing up. Men unnamed go low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on all three days and get no name from him and no word that the room was cold; he comes through with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and white in the creases of a fist that had opened, parts dusty forearms from clean as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, asks neither which slate broke nor whether it was a birth-slate. He speaks to the wheel before any person --- *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* --- tells a pot *You'll do*, knows its full note three turns ahead with air under it and a want in it and withholds the knowing on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them (T061/T082). The three shapings of the pot saying stand with the premature note from Smoke 14 on its own place and no fourth likeness made a cause; the burned-slate word from Slate Row holds at four and the shut-room tellings at two; the ownerless row question stands at five askings with no witness and no row named (T035/T031/T062, T065/T095, T101). When Sella's sentence goes low (*The fire's low. It wants---*) his bent finger goes into the cold damper ahead of it, takes the cold out of the fingertip and holds until the end arrives at the pot --- one piece of iron with two bites, mercy and mutilation in one, with no Grathic word for both, joined to no kiln (T062). *He's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn; the equality with Tarn holds at the place it moved to, neither asking about the back room or the ear. Smoke's second arithmetic stays behind and unread up the road and makes no change under the wheel-shed (T015/T041/T047). No new scar or possession, no change of voice.

Through Smoke 25–27, Days 264–266, he keeps the wheel and the household's shape at the shed's edge: meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger, dusty forearms parted from clean ones, the wheel told it does not care whose hands, only if they are steady, a pot told *You'll do* and its full note known three turns ahead and withheld on the near-full tray beside the hearings. Three or more men go low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on all three mornings and get no name from him and no word that the room was cold; his bent finger goes into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, one piece of iron with two bites and no Grathic word for both. The burned-slate word holds at four, the shut-room tellings at two and the three pot sayings at three with the premature Smoke 14 note apart; the row question takes one further shaping over the boards, a report of an asking, and stands at five askings with no witness and no row named. His promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn and *he's not Vail* stays sealed as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years. Nothing is paid, named or settled and the count goes into Smoke 28 unspent.

Through Frost 1–3, Days 270–272, he keeps the wheel and the household's shape at the shed's edge: meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger, dusty forearms parted from clean ones as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, the wheel told it does not care whose hands, only if they are steady, a pot told *You'll do* and its full note known three turns ahead and withheld on the near-full tray beside the hearings. The first firing of Frost on the 2nd is Sella's work at the kiln mouth and changes nothing under the wheel-shed; no lesson is made of it, and the boy makes no doctrine of four months' openings. His promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn and *he's not Vail* stays sealed as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years. The frost that stayed at the yard well and the plate of ice lifted from the trough on Frost 9 are yard weather entered nowhere and make no claim on the wheel; nothing under the shed is settled by them. His promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn and *he's not Vail* stays sealed as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years. Through Frost 10–12, Days 279–281, he keeps the wheel and the household's shape at the shed's edge: meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger, dusty forearms parted from clean ones as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, the wheel told it does not care whose hands, only if they are steady, a pot told *You'll do* and its full note known three turns ahead and withheld on the near-full tray beside the hearings. The burned-slate word holds at four, the shut-room tellings at two and the three pot sayings at three with the premature Smoke 14 note apart; the row question stands at five askings with one shaping and no witness and no row named. His promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn and *he's not Vail* stays sealed as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years. Through Frost 13–15, Days 282–284, he keeps the wheel and the household's shape at the shed's edge: meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger, dusty forearms parted from clean ones as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, the wheel told it does not care whose hands, only if they are steady, a pot told *You'll do* and its full note known three turns ahead and withheld on the near-full tray beside the hearings. Three or more men go low into the back room before first grey on all three mornings and get no name from him and no word that the room was cold; his bent finger goes into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, one piece of iron with two bites and no Grathic word for both. The burned-slate word holds at four, the shut-room tellings at two and the three pot sayings at three with the premature Smoke 14 note apart; the row question stands at five askings with one shaping and no witness and is not asked a sixth. The stayed frost and the plate of ice are yard weather entered nowhere and make no claim on the wheel. The Frost 14 saying at the well about the runner who carried a name in a slate is a voice arrived nowhere, kept on a place of its own beside Hessa Rale's eighth hand with a finger between, not a cause, not a confession, not a joining, not a witness and not paid; he supplies no witness to it and makes no cause of it. His promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates is neither given nor withdrawn and *he's not Vail* stays sealed as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years. Nothing is paid, named or settled and the count goes into Frost 16 unspent.

Through Bone 10–12, Days 309–311, he keeps the wheel and the household's shape at the shed's edge: meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent finger and white in the creases of a fist that had opened, dusty forearms parted from clean as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, the wheel told it does not care whose hands, only if they are steady, a pot told *You'll do* and its full note known three turns ahead and withheld on the near-full tray beside the hearings. Three or more men go low into the back room before first grey on the mornings of all three days and get no name and no word that the room was cold; his bent finger goes into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, one piece of iron with two bites and no Grathic word for both mercies. He grows quieter as the burned-slate word goes around, and the household straightens around the quiet without an order. The fifth telling stands at five with a finger between the fifth and the four, no witness standing, no hand named at the flame and no sixth due on a day that has not earned one, and the seed waits on the far-off calling-in of his promise — not a confession and not a payment. The shut-room tellings stay at two, the pot sayings at three with the Smoke 14 note apart, and the row at five askings with one shaping and no witness. Bone's first reading goes up the road in Berr's box and changes nothing under the wheel-shed; *he's not Vail* stays sealed and the promise stays ungiven. Nothing is joined, named, opened, called, paid or ended.


Through Bone 22–24, Days 321–323, he keeps the wheel-shed edge and the household straightens around his silence. Each day he came through the finger's-width door with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist had opened, not bone-coloured slip; kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray parted as clot from vessel, asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, spoke to the wheel before anyone and told a pot *You'll do* with no welcome and no unkindness. He knew the full note a pot would give three turns ahead, with air under it and a want in it, and withheld it on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them from a High Kiln master who would have called it early. Three or more men went low into the back room before first grey and got no name from him and no word that the room was cold. His bent finger went into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence on Bone 22 and 24 and the household straightened around the waiting; he read one piece of iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no Grathic word for both mercies and no slate entered. He grew quieter as the burned-slate saying went around and the household straightened around the quiet without an order; the fifth telling stood at five with a finger between the fifth and the four, no witness standing and no sixth due on a day that had not earned one, and the claim went on gathering interest against his promise to the men who burn slates, the promise neither given nor withdrawn. The shut-room tellings stood at two, the pot sayings at three with the Smoke 14 note apart, the row at five askings with no witness and the Frost 14 saying at three shapings beside Hessa Rale's eighth hand. Sella's handful at the rack end brought no word under the wheel-shed and was not joined to his dust, his damper or his promise; the finding that five readings are five and not one account stayed on a place of its own with no payer. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar, possession or change of voice.

## Chapter Log


- Ch 117: On Ilmen 8 and 9 afternoons he kept the wheel-shed edge and the household straightened around his silence without a word; offstage on Ilmen 10. He came through the finger's-width door with meal-fine grey slate dust white in a fist-crease, parted his dusty forearms from clean as clot from vessel, spoke to the wheel and a pot before any person, asked neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, knew the full note three turns ahead and withheld it, and put his bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, taking it out without the extra holding of Ash 26. A wrong wind off High Kiln on Ilmen 9 brought the sweet edge of lead glaze over the boards and out again, filed as weather with no hand or name under it. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise neither given nor withdrawn. No new scar or possession, no change of voice.
- Ch 116: On Ilmen 5 and 6 afternoons he kept the wheel-shed edge and the household straightened around his silence without a word; offstage on Ilmen 7. Before first grey on the sixth three or more men unnamed went low into the room off the wheel-shed, keeping its finger's width of open and its sound of low voices, and he gave them no name and no word that the room was cold. He came through the finger's-width door with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and white in a fist-crease where a hand had opened, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; he kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and parted them as clot from vessel, asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, while the broken ones under the lowest rack stayed entered by nobody (T035/T031/T062 advanced, not paid). He spoke to the wheel and a pot before any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note three turns ahead with air under it and a want in it and withheld the knowing from any High Kiln master who would call it early and sing a pot before its time. His bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, took the cold out of the fingertip, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and was left standing so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot; he came out without leaving the extra holding of Ash 26 a second time — one iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no Grathic word covering both mercies. That day a man from Slate Row whose name was on no roll stood at the yard's edge with his hands loose; the boy filed the face on its own tray and joined neither the waiting nor the men unnamed to the promise under the shed, the burned slate at five or the four directions, for a doctrine is an account that has found a payer and none stood. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name neither given nor withdrawn while the burned slate stood at five, the shut-room tellings at two, the row at five askings with no witness, the well-saying at four shapings and the Ash and Slate directions held apart and not repeated. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar, wound, rank or possession and no change of voice.

He spoke to the wheel before he spoke to any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — and told a pot *You'll do* with no welcome and no unkindness either, knowing the full note it would give three turns before it left his hands, with air under it and a want in it, and withholding the knowing from any master come up from High Kiln who would call it early. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of her sentence, took the cold out of the fingertip, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and was left standing so that the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around the waiting — one piece of iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no Grathic word covering both mercies. The finger came out without leaving the extra holding of Ash 26 a second time. With Tarn the equality held where it moved to, his finger standing in the damper while Tarn's hand went up and away at the sill with no breath held at the top of the reach on all three days, the two silences unjoined and the loan uncalled. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise neither given nor withdrawn while the fourth ownerless direction over the boards on Stone 23 — a room shut against the cold and a slate burned so a count would not go up — gathered interest against it and was expressly not joined to it; the burned slate stood at five, the shut-room tellings at two and the Ash 5 joining and the Ash 17 and Ash 23 directions apart and unspent. No wound, growth, rank, power, scar or possession, no change of voice; the promise is still the betrayal waiting to be called. Forward, not closed.

- Ch 109: Days 378, 379 and 380, Stone 19–21, Ninthday to Firstday, at the wheel-shed edge and the cold damper in the afternoons, last seen on the afternoon of the twenty-first at the cold damper. Dusty forearms and clean ones were kept on one tray and parted as clot from vessel, asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate while the broken ones lay under the lowest rack entered by nobody. He spoke to the wheel before he spoke to any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — and told a pot *You'll do* with no welcome and no unkindness either, knowing the full note it would give three turns before it left his hands, with air under it and a want in it, and withholding the knowing from any master come up from High Kiln who would call it early. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of her sentence, took the cold out of the fingertip, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and was left standing so that the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around the waiting — one piece of iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no Grathic word covering both mercies. The finger came out without leaving the extra holding of Ash 26 a second time; that holding had been gathered once and stayed where nobody entered it. With Tarn the equality held where it moved to, his finger standing in the damper while Tarn's hand went up and away at the sill with no breath held at the top of the reach on the twenty-first, the two silences unjoined and the loan uncalled. With Corin nothing changed: the boy lay under the roof unwritten, *he's not Vail* stayed sealed as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years, and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn, gathering interest against the burned slate at five, the shut-room tellings at two, the row at five askings and the Ash 5, 17 and 23 directions. Eleven arithmetics going up the clay road in a locked box brought no loosening of the promise and no word under the shed, and the keeping at the fold found where put on six mornings and six goings of the light brought neither. No wound, growth, rank, power, scar or possession, no change of voice; the promise is still the betrayal waiting to be called. Forward, not closed.

- Ch 108: Days 375, 376 and 377, Stone 16–18, Sixthday to Eighthday, last seen at the wheel-shed edge and the cold damper on the afternoon of the eighteenth. It cares if they're steady* — and told a pot *You'll do* with no welcome and no unkindness, knowing the full note three turns ahead and withholding it from any High Kiln master who would call it early. His bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence — one iron with two bites, the holding and the taking — and came out without leaving the extra holding of Ash 26 a second time. With Tarn the equality held where it moved to, his finger standing in the damper while the broom went on over the flagstones without the half-check, the two silences unjoined and the loan uncalled. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years; the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn and went on gathering interest against the burned slate at five, the shut-room tellings at two, the row at five askings and the Ash 5, 17 and 23 directions. No wound, growth, rank, power, scar or possession, no change of voice; the promise is still the betrayal waiting to be called. Forward, not closed.

- Ch 107: Days 372, 373 and 374, Stone 13–15, Thirdday to Fifthday, at the wheel-shed edge and the cold damper in the afternoons, last seen in the afternoon of the fifteenth at the cold damper. He came through the finger's-width door off the wheel-shed each afternoon with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and white in a fist-crease where a hand had opened, and it was not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; he kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and parted them as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate while the broken ones lay under the lowest rack and were entered by nobody. Three or more men unnamed went low into the back room before first grey and got no name from him and no word that the room was cold. He spoke to the wheel before he spoke to any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — and told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome in it and no unkindness either, knowing the full note it would give three turns before it left his hands, with air under it and a want in it, and kept the knowing where nobody could make a song of it and withheld it from any master come up from High Kiln who would call it early and sing a pot before its time. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of her sentence, took the cold out of the fingertip, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and was left standing so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around the waiting as cloth straightens when a weight is lifted — one piece of iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no Grathic word covering both mercies. The finger came out without leaving the extra holding of Ash 26 a second time, gathered once, found where put and not made again, not a sixth, seventh or eighth held object, not a habit, a way, a sign, a hearing, a mending, a cause, a payment or a rule, with no payer standing. Sella's unfinished sentence still arrived at the pot and he did not look toward the bed or the basket and did not answer with a word; she asked nothing about the men, the dust, the damper or the small keeping she left at the fold on the fifteenth evening. With Tarn the equality held where it had moved to: his bent finger stood in the cold damper while Tarn's stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check, the two silences unjoined and the loan uncalled. With Corin nothing changed: the boy lay under the roof unwritten, *he's not Vail* stayed sealed as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years, and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn, gathering interest against the burned slate at five, the shut-room tellings at two, the row at five askings, the four rooms with the same gap and the Ash 5, 17 and 23 directions, buying no lesson from three ordinary days. No bond changed hands, no name given, no slate opened, no new scar or possession and no change of voice; the promise is still uncalled. Forward, not closed.

- Ch 106: Days 369, 370 and 371, Stone 10–12, Tenthday to Secondday, on stage at the wheel-shed edge and the damper on the afternoons, the household straightening around his silence without a word from anybody; nothing from the tenth arithmetic at the Cross or the Hearth Drawing two doors down crossed under the wheel-shed as sign, lesson, mending, cause, payment or payer. He came through the finger's-width door off the wheel-shed with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and white in a fist-crease where a hand had opened, and it was not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; kept his dusty forearms and his clean ones on one tray and parted them as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it had been a birth-slate while the broken ones lay under the lowest rack and were entered by nobody. Three or more men unnamed had gone low into the back room before first grey and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold. He spoke to the wheel before he spoke to any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — and told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome in it and no unkindness either, knowing the full note it would give three turns before it left his hands, with air under it and a want in it, and kept the knowing where nobody could make a song of it, withheld from any master come up from High Kiln who would call it early and sing a pot before its time; no seventh hearing stood up in the empty place. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low, his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of her sentence, took the cold out of the fingertip, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and was left standing so that the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around the waiting as cloth straightens when a weight is lifted; one piece of iron had two bites, the holding and the taking, and there was no Grathic word for both mercies, and the boy read the two bites as anatomy under pressure — the flow held and the flow stopped in the same iron, mercy and mutilation in one bite — then the finger came out without leaving the extra holding of Ash 26 a second time, gathered once, found where put and not made again, for a second would be a habit and a third a way and both want a payer and none stood. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates stayed neither given nor withdrawn, gathering interest against the burned slate at five, the shut-room tellings at two, the row at five askings and the three Ash directions held apart. Ten arithmetics went up the clay road in Berr's locked box and changed nothing under the wheel-shed; the Drawing collector and his grey fingertips belonged to another house and no hand of his was filed among the record-first hands. With Tarn the equality held at the place it moved to, neither asking about the back room or the ear, and the loan stayed uncalled. No new scar or possession, no change of voice, no bond paid or spoken, no ending. Forward, not closed.

- Ch 105: Days 366, 367 and 368, Stone 7–9, Seventhday to Ninthday, on stage at the wheel-shed edge and the damper on the afternoons, the household straightening around his silence without a word from anybody. He came through the finger's-width door off the wheel-shed with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and white in a fist-crease where a hand had opened, and it was not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; kept his dusty forearms and his clean ones on one tray and parted them as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it had been a birth-slate while the broken ones lay under the lowest rack and were entered by nobody. Three or more men unnamed had gone low into the back room before first grey and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold. He spoke to the wheel before he spoke to any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — and told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome in it and no unkindness either, knowing the full note it would give three turns before it left his hands, with air under it and a want in it, and kept the knowing where nobody could make a song of it, withheld from any master come up from High Kiln who would call it early and sing a pot before its time; no seventh hearing stood up in the empty place. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low, his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of her sentence, took the cold out of the fingertip, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and was left standing so that the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around the waiting as cloth straightens when a weight is lifted; one piece of iron had two bites, the holding and the taking, and there was no Grathic word for both mercies, and the boy read the two bites as anatomy under pressure — the flow held and the flow stopped in the same iron, mercy and mutilation in one bite, with a price that had to be named somewhere or be paid by somebody who had not agreed to pay it. Then he took the finger out without leaving the extra holding of Ash 26 a second time; it stayed gathered once on a place of its own with no payer under it, for a second would be a habit and a third a way. The boy filed the dust, the two bites of iron and the unspent breath on separate places and wrote no cause across any of them. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years, and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn, gathering interest on its own place under the shed and buying no lesson from the unmarked birthday or the year that finished. The burned slate stood at five, the shut-room tellings at two, the row at five askings and the Ash 5 joining, Ash 17 direction and Ash 23 direction apart and unspent; no ownerless voice came over the boards on any of the three days to make a sixth telling, a third telling, a sixth asking, a fifth shaping or a fourth direction, and no witness stood. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar or possession and no change of voice.

- Ch 101: Days 354, 355 and 356, Ash 25–27, Fifthday to Seventhday, on stage at the wheel-shed edge and the damper on the twenty-fifth, twenty-sixth and twenty-seventh. Came through the finger's-width door with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and white in a fist-crease where a hand had opened, and it was not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and parted them as clot from vessel, asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate; men unnamed went low into the back room before first grey and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold. Spoke to the wheel before any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — and told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness either; knew the full note that pot would give three turns ahead, with air under it and a want in it, and withheld the knowing from any High Kiln master who would call it early and make a song of it. Put his bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, took the cold out of the fingertip, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing, so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around the waiting as cloth straightens when a weight is lifted; one piece of iron had two bites, the holding and the taking, and no Grathic word for both mercies. **The chapter's one meaningful change, advanced and NOT paid (T062/T035/T031 and T061/T082):** on Ash 26, after the tongue had been moved, the sentence finished and the household straightened around the waiting — after the work was done, not before — the bent finger stayed in the cold iron a breath longer than the work required. He made no song of it, named it to nobody, did not look toward the basket, gave no name to the men in the back room and did not say the room was cold. The boy filed the extra interval on a place of its own with a finger between it and the five held objects, the pot-crack resting, the hand-at-the-sill breath and his own gaps — a private keeping gathered once and not spent, expressly not a sixth held object, not a seventh, not an eighth of anything, not a habit, not a way, not a hearing, not a near-full answer, not a mending, not a cause, not a payment and not a rule, with no payer standing under it — gathering toward the far-off hand that will one day lay the district's private keepings together, and that hand not his; the boy did not join it to the dust, the damper's double bite, the uncalled debt, the covered grain, the charcoal, Ilva's strip or the Ash 23 direction, the edges coming near to touching and the finger going between. On Ash 27 he took his finger out and left the interval where he had put it and did not make it again, for a second would be a habit and a third a way and both want a payer standing under them and none stood; he did not check whether anybody had noticed. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn while the claim gathered interest against it; the burned slate stood at five, the shut-room tellings at two, the row at five askings and the Ash 5, Ash 17 and Ash 23 directions held apart with a finger between every pair. Eight arithmetics stood behind one unsigned figure up the road and brought no word under the wheel-shed. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar or possession and no change of voice.

The direction did not come again on the twenty-fourth and stood where it was put, gathering interest against his promise. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn; eight arithmetics stood behind one unsigned figure up the road and brought no word under the wheel-shed. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar or possession and no change of voice.

- Ch 98: Days 345, 346 and 347, Ash 16–18, Sixthday to Eighthday, on stage at the shed's edge and the damper on all three days. Came through the finger's-width door off the Vail wheel-shed with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and white in the creases of a fist that had opened, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; parted dusty forearms from clean as clot from vessel; asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate; men unnamed went low into the back room before first grey and got no name from him and he did not say the room was cold. Tarn's held breath at the knife-sill brought no word under the shed and was not laid across any of these; Ilva's ninth hand at the well on Ash 17 stayed nine with the strip unread and she was expressly not the speaker. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn while the claim went on gathering interest against it; seven arithmetics stood behind one unsigned figure up the road with Ash's first locked away and Ash's second two mornings off. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar or possession and no change of voice.

The burned slate stood at five with no witness and no sixth, the shut-room tellings at two, the row at five askings, and no ownerless saying came in these three days; the Ash 5 joining stayed held apart (T035/T031/T065/T095 held). - Ch 94: Days 333–335, Ash 4–6, Fourthday to Sixthday, at the shed's edge and the damper. Came through the finger's-width door each day with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and white in a fist-crease, not bone-coloured slip; parted dusty forearms from clean as clot from vessel; asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate; three or more men went low into the back room before first grey and got no name from him and he did not say the room was cold. Spoke to the wheel before any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — and told a pot *You'll do*; knew the full note three turns ahead with air under it and a want in it and withheld it from any High Kiln master who would call it early and make a song of it; read the damper as one iron with two bites and no Grathic word for both mercies; put the bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the end of it arrived at the pot. **On Ash 5 he grew quieter as the ownerless joining of the burned slate to the shut room went around the boards and the household straightened around the quiet without an order**; the burned slate stayed at five with no witness and no sixth, the shut-room tellings at two, and the claim went on gathering interest against his promise, neither given nor withdrawn. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar or possession and no change of voice.

- Ch 93: Days 330, 331 and 332, Ash 1–3, Firstday to Thirdday, on stage at the shed's edge and the damper on all three days. Came through the finger's-width door off the Vail wheel-shed with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and white in a fist-crease where a hand had opened, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and parted them as clot from vessel, asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, while the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack stayed entered by nobody. Spoke to the wheel before any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — and told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome in it and no unkindness either. Knew the full note that pot would give three turns ahead, with air under it and a want in it, and withheld the knowing from any High Kiln master who would call it early and make a song of it, keeping it where nobody could make a song of it (T061/T082 advanced, not paid). Three or more men went low into the back room before first grey on each of the three mornings and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold; the shut-room tellings stayed at two with no third (T035/T031/T065/T095 held). Before Sella had finished telling him the fire was low his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper, took the cold out of the fingertip, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and was left standing so that the end of her instruction arrived at the pot, and the household straightened around his silence; he read one piece of iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no Grathic word for both mercies and no slate entered (T035/T031/T062). He grew quieter whenever the burned-slate word went around and nobody gave him an order to stop; the telling stood at five with a finger between the fifth and the four, no witness standing, no hand named at any flame and no sixth due on a day that had not earned one, and the claim went on gathering interest against his promise (T035/T031/T065/T095 advanced, not paid). The row stood at five askings with no witness, the well-saying at three shapings, the nine stoppings with no tenth and the loan uncalled. Six arithmetics of the year stood behind one printed figure in Berr's locked box up the road, one in twelve unchallenged, the figure read still not the figure paid, no signature on the space, six readings six and not one account; the finding sat on a place of its own with no payer and was not laid across his dust, his damper, the covered grain, the four held changes, Sella's handful, the sixth opening or the boy's gaps. The sixth opening at the kiln mouth on Ash 2 came and went without a word from him: six openings in six months are six and not a rule and no seventh is made due (T058/T079, T088 advanced, not paid). *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. With Tarn the two silences stayed unjoined and neither asked about the ear or the room. No collector came up or went down the clay road, no bond changed hands, no new scar or possession and no change of voice.

Three or more men went low into the back room before first grey on the three mornings and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold; the shut-room tellings stayed at two with no third (T035/T031/T065/T095 held). He grew quieter whenever the burned-slate word went around and nobody gave him an order to stop; the telling stood at five with a finger between the fifth and the four, no witness standing, no hand named at any flame and no sixth due on a day that had not earned one. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years, and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn; the knowing was kept where nobody could make a song of it. No bond changed hands, no new scar or possession and no change of voice.

No collector came up or went down the clay road, no new scar or possession and no change of voice.

No bond paid or spoken, no new scar, possession or change of voice.
