Tarn's stroke went over the bright patch without the half-check and he neither asked about the ear nor was asked about the room; the two silences stayed unjoined and the loan that can hear was not called. The boy's staying at the inch on the fifteenth was not brought under the wheel-shed as sign, lesson, mending, cause or payment; his six answers, the hundred and ninety-ninth through the two hundred and fourth, stayed objects on a place of its own, and the finding of four arithmetics behind one printed figure was kept apart from *he's not Vail* and the promise. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar or possession, no change of voice.

- Ch 86: Days 309, 310 and 311, Bone 10–12, Tenthday to Secondday, on stage at the edge of the Vail wheel-shed on all three days. Came through the finger's-width door each morning with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist had opened, not bone-coloured slip; kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and parted them as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, asking neither which slate had broken nor whether it had been a birth-slate. Spoke to the wheel before any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — and told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness. Knew the full note that pot would give three turns before it left his hands, with air under it and a want in it, and withheld the knowing on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them, and would not say it before a kiln-master out of High Kiln who would call it early and make something of it. Before Sella had finished telling him the fire was low his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot; the boy read one piece of iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no Grathic word for both mercies and no slate entered. Three or more men unnamed went low into the back room before first grey and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold; the sound arrived at the basket as a direction. He grew quieter as the saying about the burned slate went around and the household straightened around the quiet without an order; the fifth telling stood at five with a finger between the fifth and the four, no witness standing to be asked, no hand named at any flame and no sixth due on a day that had not earned one, and the claim went on gathering interest against his promise to the men who burn slates, the promise neither given nor withdrawn. The shut-room tellings stood at two and not three, the three pot sayings at three with the Smoke 14 note on its own place apart, the row at five askings with one shaping and no witness. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name neither given nor withdrawn. He did not look toward the bed or the basket. No new scar, possession or change of voice; no bond paid or spoken.

- Ch 85: Days 306, 307 and 308, Bone 7–9, Seventhday to Ninthday, on stage at the edge of the Vail wheel-shed on all three days. Came through the finger's-width-open door each day with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bend of his little finger and under the nails, not bone-coloured slip; kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray, parted as clot from vessel, asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it had been a birth-slate. Spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*) and told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness; knew its full note three turns ahead with air under it and a want in it and withheld it on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them from a High Kiln master who would have called it early. Men unnamed went low into the back room before first grey on the mornings of all three days and got no name and no word that the room was cold. His bent finger went into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence and the household straightened around his silence. **The chapter's one meaningful change:** at the going of the light on Bone 8 he left the finger standing in the cold iron while she let the end of her sentence arrive at the pot; Tarn's broom checked half a beat behind the standing finger on the flagstones and went on toward the road without a word or a turn of the head, the Seed 22 half-check and the Day 171 flagstone movement returned changed after a hundred and thirty-six days. Neither man named the other's silence and the loan that can hear was not called; the boy filed the returned half-check as the fourth held change on a fourth place and would not let four such changes stand up as a rule. The burned slate stood at five with a finger between it and the four, the shut-room tellings at two, the three pot sayings with the Smoke 14 note apart, the row at five askings with no witness and the Frost 14 saying at three shapings beside Hessa Rale's eighth hand. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the foundling's name neither given nor withdrawn. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar or possession, no change of voice.

- Ch 84: Days 303, 304 and 305, Bone 4–6, Fourthday to Sixthday, on stage at the edge of the Vail wheel-shed on all three days. Came through the finger's-width-open door each day with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bend of his little finger and under the nails, not bone-coloured slip; kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray, parted as clot from vessel, asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it had been a birth-slate. Spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*) and told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness; knew its full note three turns ahead with air under it and a want in it and withheld it on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them. Men unnamed went low into the back room before first grey on the mornings of all three days and got no name and no word that the room was cold; to the boy it was a direction and a sound. His bent finger went into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence and the household straightened around his silence; the boy read one iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, joined to no kiln and entered in no slate. **The chapter's one meaningful change:** on Bone 5 an ownerless voice came up from Slate Row over the boards of the Clay Yard, on the same breath as the price of barley and the weather, and said a slate had burned again; there was no owner standing under it, no room, no hand at the flame, no reason and no witness. He grew quieter whenever the saying went around the boards, made no sixth of the fifth and no cause of the likeness, and the boy filed it as the fifth telling of the burned slate with a finger between it and the four — still not a third telling of the counting room shut in the war, which stands at two. The burned slate stood at five, the shut-room tellings at two, the three pot sayings with the Smoke 14 note apart, the row at five askings with one shaping and no witness, and the Frost 14 saying at three shapings beside Hessa Rale's eighth hand with a finger between every pair. Sella's proverbs to the twice-mended pot and the crack left showing brought no lesson under the roof. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar or possession, no change of voice.

- Ch 83: Days 300, 301 and 302, Bone 1–3, Firstday to Thirdday, on stage at the edge of the Vail wheel-shed on all three days. Came through the finger's-width-open door each day with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bend of his little finger and under the nails, not bone-coloured slip; kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray, parted as clot from vessel, asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. Spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*) and told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness; knew its full note three turns ahead with air under it and a want in it and withheld it on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them, advanced and not paid. Men unnamed went low into the back room before first grey on the mornings of Days 300, 301 and 302 and got no name and no word that the room was cold; the burned slate stood at four, the shut-room tellings at two, the three pot sayings with the Smoke 14 note apart, and the row at five askings with one shaping and no witness. His bent finger went into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence and the household straightened around his silence. The Frost 14 saying stood at three shapings on a place of its own beside Hessa Rale's eighth hand with a finger between every pair, not a witness, not a cause, not a confession, not a joining and not paid, and not making the runner a reader. Sella's opening before the kiln on Bone 2 and the went-off white on the well lip brought no lesson under the roof. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the foundling's name neither given nor withdrawn. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar or possession, no change of voice.

It cares if they're steady*) and told a pot *You'll do* with no welcome and no unkindness; knew its full note three turns ahead with air under it and a want in it and withheld it. Men unnamed went low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on all three mornings and got no name and no word that the room was cold; the burned slate stayed at four, the shut-room tellings at two, the pot sayings at three with the Smoke 14 note apart, and the row at five askings with one shaping and no witness. His bent finger went into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence and the household straightened around his silence. The Frost 14 saying gained a third shaping from the ownerless voice over the boards and the well-roof on Frost 23 and was kept on a place of its own beside Hessa Rale's eighth hand with a finger between every pair, not a witness, not a cause, not a confession, not a joining and not paid. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the foundling's name neither given nor withdrawn. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar or possession, no change of voice.

- Ch 78: Days 285, 286 and 287, Frost 16–18, Sixthday to Eighthday, on stage at the edge of the Vail wheel-shed on all three days. Came through with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bend of his little finger and under the nails, not bone-coloured *slip*; kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and parted them as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, while the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack were entered by nobody (T009/T013/T092). Spoke to the wheel before he spoke to any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness, knew its full note three turns before it left his hands with air under it and a want in it, and withheld the knowing on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them, advanced and not paid (T061/T082). Three pot sayings stayed three with the premature Smoke 14 note on its own place and no fourth likeness made a cause, no pot lost and no name given to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188. Burned slate stood at four from Slate Row and the two shut-room tellings at two, with no fifth and no third; the ownerless row question stood at five askings with one shaping and no witness, a silence kept and not a sixth witness, and was not asked a sixth time (T035/T031/T065/T095, T101 held). Three or more men went low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on the mornings of Days 285, 286 and 287, got no name from him and no word that the room was cold; the room's sound arrived at the basket as a direction. When Sella's sentence came low and unfinished his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of the end of it, took the cold out of the fingertip, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence — one iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, joined to no kiln and entered in no slate (T062/T035/T031). *He's not Vail* stayed sealed, the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn, and nothing of the Frost 14 saying was joined to any of it; no new scar, possession or change of voice.

- Ch 77: Days 282, 283 and 284, Frost 13–15, Thirdday to Fifthday, on stage at the edge of the Vail wheel-shed on all three days. Came through with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bend of his little finger and under the nails, white in the creases of a fist that had opened, not bone-coloured *slip*; kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and parted them as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, while the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack were entered by nobody (T009/T013/T092). Spoke to the wheel before he spoke to any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness, knew its full note three turns before it left his hands with air under it and a want in it, and withheld the knowing on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them, advanced and not paid (T061/T082). Three pot sayings stayed three with the premature Smoke 14 note on its own place and no fourth likeness made a cause, no pot lost and no name given to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188. Burned slate stood at four from Slate Row and the two shut-room tellings at two, with no fifth and no third; the ownerless row question stood at five askings with one shaping and no witness, a silence kept and not a sixth witness, and was not asked a sixth time (T035/T031/T065/T095, T101 held). Three or more men went low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on the mornings of Days 282, 283 and 284, got no name from him and no word that the room was cold; the room's sound arrived at the basket as a direction. When Sella's sentence came low and unfinished his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of the end of it, took the cold out of the fingertip, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence; the boy read one piece of iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no Grathic word for both mercies, joined to no kiln and entered in no slate (T062/T035). Frost's first arithmetic stayed up the clay road in Berr's locked box and changed nothing under the wheel-shed; no lesson was made of it, the first firing of Frost stayed behind the household with no payer and the boy made no doctrine of four months' openings (T015/T041/T047, T058/T079 held). The stayed frost and the plate of ice were yard weather entered nowhere and made no claim on the wheel (T083/T085 held). The Frost 14 saying at the well was a voice arrived nowhere with no owner to be asked; he did not claim it, supply a witness to it or make a cause of it, and it stood on a place of its own beside Hessa Rale's eighth hand with a finger between (T080 advanced, not paid). With Corin nothing passed: the boy lay under the roof unwritten, his six answers stayed objects on a sixth place, and the finding that a house keeps by not-doings was held apart from the back room's omissions with a finger between. With Tarn the equality held at the place it had moved to, neither asking about the ear or the room, his bent finger in the damper while the debt's stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check. His promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn and *he's not Vail* stayed sealed as a lid nobody has tried to lift in three years. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar or possession, no change of voice.

- Ch 63: Days 240, 241 and 242, Smoke 1–3, Firstday to Thirdday, on stage at the wheel-shed edge, the cold damper, the kiln mouth, the drying racks and the Clay Yard on all three days. Came through with the day's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist had opened, not bone-coloured slip; parted dust from slip as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, while the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack were entered by nobody (T009/T013/T092). Three or more men unnamed went low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on all three mornings and got no name from him, and he did not say the room was cold; the two tellings of the room shut in the war stayed two with no third (T035/T031, T065/T095). Spoke to the wheel before he spoke to any person — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome in it and no unkindness either, knew its full note three turns before it left his hands with air under it and a want in it, and withheld the knowing on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them, advanced and not paid (T061/T082). The three shapings of the pot saying stood as sayings — sings itself out early, not heard from since, gone quiet on every row come the thaw — and on Smoke 3 a wrong wind off High Kiln brought the sweet edge of lead glaze over the boards, touched the fold and went out on it; he let one of the sayings stand again as a saying, made no lesson of the green, lost no pot and gave no name to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188 (T064 held, T077). The burned-slate word held at four tellings out of Slate Row, still a direction arrived nowhere and still not a third telling of the counting room shut in the war; the row question stood on its seventh place with four askings, no owner, room, hand at the kiln, cause, witness or row named, and he did not claim it, supply a witness or make a cause of it (T101 carried). When Sella's sentence went low and unfinished at the hearth his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of it, took the cold out of the fingertip, held until the end of the sentence arrived at the pot, moved a hair's breadth and was left standing — a remembered repetition and not a change made again, paired with no other fraction. The iron stayed one piece with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no word in Grathic for both mercies, joined to no kiln and entered in no slate (T062). He did not ask about the ear while Tarn did not ask about the room, and the two silences stayed unjoined. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as on the hour it was spoken, and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. The dressed grain stayed covered under wool with no figure and the returning-line place went around with a look and no number; no new scar, possession or change of voice, no bond paid or spoken.

- Ch 62: Days 237, 238 and 239, Grain 28–30, Eighthday to Tenthday, on stage at the wheel-shed edge, the cold damper, the kiln mouth and the Clay Yard on all three days. Came through with the morning's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist had opened, not bone-coloured slip; parted dusty forearms from clean ones on one tray as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, kept them there and asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate; the broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack where the yard went around them and entered them into nothing (T009/T013/T092). Three or more men unnamed went low and quiet into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on all three mornings and got no name from him, and he did not say the room was cold; the two tellings of the room shut in the war stayed two with no third (T035/T031, T065/T095). Spoke to the wheel before he spoke to anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness, knew its full note three turns before it left his hands with air under it and a want in it, and withheld the knowing on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them (T061/T082). The three shapings of the pot saying stayed sayings — sings itself out early, not heard from since, gone quiet on every row come the thaw — with no fourth likeness made a cause, no pot lost and no name given to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188; the burned-slate word stood at four and the shut-room tellings at two with no fifth and no third (T035/T031/T065/T095 held, not paid). When Sella's sentence went low his bent finger went into the cold damper ahead of it, took the cold out of the fingertip, held until the end of the sentence arrived at the pot, moved a hair's breadth and was left standing — a remembered repetition and not a change made again, paired with no other fraction and joined to no kiln (T062). **The Grain 21 question gained a fourth asking** on the afternoon of Grain 29: a voice with no owner, no room, no hand at the kiln and no cause came over the boards low into the boards and not into the basket, the weather answered first and the price of barley after it, the voice went off up the terrace toward High Kiln and the row stood where it stood with nobody leaning in to take the answer down. The boy set the fourth telling on the seventh place beside the first three with a finger between each pair; four askings are still not one witness, and the row was not named and no fifth asking came (T101 advanced, not paid). *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn; the charcoal beneath the loaf shelf stayed unread and the private keeping unshown. No new scar or possession, no change of voice, no bond paid or spoken; the burned-slate word went on gathering interest at four and the count crossed into Smoke 1 unspent.

- Ch 61: Days 234, 235 and 236, Grain 25–27, Fifthday to Seventhday, on stage at the wheel-shed edge, the cold damper, the kiln mouth and the Clay Yard on all three days. Came through with the morning's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist had opened, not bone-coloured slip; parted dusty forearms from clean ones as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, kept them on one tray and asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate; the broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack where the yard went around them and entered them into nothing (T009/T013/T092). Three or more men unnamed went low and quiet into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on all three mornings and got no name from him, and he did not say the room was cold; the two tellings of the room shut in the war stayed two with no third (T035/T031, T065/T095). Spoke to the wheel before he spoke to anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness, knew its full note three turns before it left his hands with air under it and a want in it, and withheld the knowing on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them (T061/T082). The three shapings of the pot saying stayed sayings — sings itself out early, not heard from since, gone quiet on every row come the thaw — with no fourth likeness made a cause, no pot lost and no name given to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188; the burned-slate word stood at four and the shut-room tellings at two with no fifth and no third (T035/T031/T065/T095 held, not paid). **The Grain 21 question gained a third asking** on the afternoon of Grain 26: a voice with no owner went over the boards, the weather answered and the price of barley after it, the voice went up the terrace and the row stood where it stood; he filed the third telling on its own seventh place beside the first two with a finger between each pair and fingers between them and the sayings, the slate, the shut room, the hearings and the count. Three askings are not one witness; he supplied no witness, named no row and made no cause of it (T101 advanced, not paid). His quiet went before him into the shed and the household straightened around it without an order; a wrong wind off High Kiln brought the sweet edge of lead glaze over the boards on all three days, touched the fold and went out on the wind that brought it, a direction and not a second edge paid, the seventh green still a colour and the seventh shape still a shape with no eighth (T064, T050/T051 and T033/T034 held). His bent finger went into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, took the cold out of the fingertip and gave nothing back, held until the sentence reached the pot, moved a hair's breadth and was left standing — a remembered repetition and not a change made again, joined to no kiln and paired with no other fraction (T062). *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn; the dressed grain stayed under wool with no figure and the returning-line place went around with a look and no number. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar, possession or change of voice.

- Ch 60: Days 231, 232 and 233, Grain 22–24, Secondday to Fourthday, on stage at the wheel-shed edge, the cold damper, the kiln mouth and the Clay Yard on all three days. Came through with the morning's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist had opened, not bone-coloured slip; parted dusty forearms from clean ones as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, kept them on one tray and asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate; the broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack where the yard went around them and entered them into nothing (T009/T013/T092). Three or more men unnamed went low and quiet into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on all three mornings and got no name from him, and he did not say the room was cold; the two tellings of the room shut in the war stayed two with no third (T035/T031, T065/T095). Spoke to the wheel before he spoke to anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness, knew its full note three turns before it left his hands with air under it and a want in it, and withheld the knowing on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them (T061/T082). The three shapings of the pot saying stayed sayings — sings itself out early, not heard from since, gone quiet on every row come the thaw — with no fourth likeness made a cause, no pot lost and no name given to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188; the burned-slate word stood at four and the shut-room tellings at two with no fifth and no third (T035/T031/T065/T095 held, not paid). **The Grain 21 question gained a second asking** on the afternoon of Grain 23: a voice with no owner went over the boards, the weather answered and the price of barley after it, the voice went up the terrace and the row stood where it stood; he filed it on its own seventh place beside the first with a finger between the two and fingers between it and the sayings, the slate, the shut-room tellings, the six hearings and the count. Two askings are not one witness; he supplied no witness, named no row, made no cause and let it stand as a seed for the far-off day a witness stands somewhere and can be asked (T101 advanced, not paid). He grew quieter whenever the burned-slate word went around and the household straightened around the quiet without an order, the quiet going before him into the shed the way a cold air goes before a door. When Sella's voice went low and unfinished at the hearth his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of her sentence, took the cold out of the fingertip and gave nothing back, held until the sentence reached the pot, moved a hair's breadth and was left standing — a remembered repetition and not a change made again, joined to no kiln and paired with no other fraction; the iron was one piece with two bites, with no word in Grathic for both mercies (T062). *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years, and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn; he did not look toward the bed or the basket. The dressed grain stayed covered under wool with no figure, the returning-line place was gone around with a look and no number, and Berr, Hessa and the clerk stayed offstage. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar, possession or change of voice.

- Ch 59: Days 228, 229 and 230, Grain 19–21, Ninthday to Firstday, on stage at the wheel-shed edge, the cold damper, the kiln mouth and the Clay Yard on all three days. Came through with the morning's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist had opened, not bone-coloured slip; parted dusty forearms from clean ones as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, kept them on one tray and asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate; the broken birth-slates lay under the lowest rack where the yard went around them and entered them into nothing (T009/T013/T092). Spoke to the wheel before he spoke to anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness, knew its full note three turns before it left his hands, with air under it and a want in it, and kept the knowing back on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them (T061/T082). Three or more men unnamed went low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on all three mornings and got no name from him, and he did not say the room was cold; the two tellings of the room shut in the war stayed two with no third (T035/T031, T065/T095). The burned-slate word had come a fourth time over the boards from Slate Row and arrived nowhere, a direction with no hand at the flame and no figure at its foot; he grew quieter whenever it went around and the household straightened around the quiet without an order, the quiet going before him into the shed the way a cold air goes before a door. No fifth telling came and no third telling of the shut room (T035/T031/T065/T095 held, not paid). A wrong-wind kiln-hand come up off High Kiln let one of the three shapings stand over the boards on Grain 21 — *a pot gone quiet on every row come the thaw* — and went on to the weather and the price of barley; he let it stand as a saying and no more, taking it apart and setting it back, with no fourth likeness made a cause, no lesson, no name for what ailed the man carried out on Day 188 and no pot lost; the sweet edge of lead glaze came up on the wrong wind, touched the fold and went out on the wind that brought it, a direction and not a second edge paid (T064). When Sella's voice went low and unfinished at the hearth his bent finger went into the cold damper ahead of her sentence and stood there, the iron taking the cold out of the fingertip and giving nothing back; he held until the sentence reached the pot, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing — a remembered repetition, not a change made again, joined to no kiln and entered in no slate, one iron with two bites and no Grathic word for both mercies (T062). He did not pair it with the Day 161 fraction, the Day 166 beat or the Day 171 half-check; three places were three places and not a rule with a payer standing to any. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as the hour it was spoken and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. On Grain 20 he was offstage at the market. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar, possession or change of voice.

- Ch 58: Days 225, 226 and 227, Grain 16–18, Sixthday to Eighthday, on stage at the wheel-shed edge, the cold damper, the kiln mouth and the Clay Yard on all three days. Came through with the morning's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist had opened, not bone-coloured slip; parted dusty forearms from clean ones as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, kept them on one tray and asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, while the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack stayed entered by nobody (T009/T013/T092). Spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness, knew its full note three turns before it left his hands, with air under it and a want in it, and kept the knowing back on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them (T061/T082). Three or more men unnamed went low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on the mornings he was seen and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold; the two tellings of the room shut in the war stayed two and no third came (T035/T031, T065/T095). The burned-slate word had come over the boards four times out of Slate Row and arrived nowhere, a direction with no hand at the flame and no figure at its foot; he grew quieter whenever it went around and the household straightened around the quiet without an order, the quiet going before him into the shed the way a cold air goes before a door. No fifth telling came and no third telling of the shut room (T035/T031/T065/T095 held, not paid). A kiln-hand come up off High Kiln on a wrong wind let one of the three shapings of the pot saying stand over the boards --- a pot gone quiet on every row come the thaw --- and went on to the price of barley and the weather, giving no owner, room, hand at the flame, figure or reason; the sweetish green edge of lead glaze came up on that wind, touched the fold and went out on the wind that brought it, a direction and not a second edge paid, while the seventh green stayed a colour with no eighth (T061/T082, T064, T050/T051 held). No pot was lost, no fourth likeness made a cause, no collector called and no name given to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188. When Sella's sentence went low his bent finger went into the cold damper and held until the sentence reached the pot, then moved a hair's breadth and was left standing --- a remembered repetition, not a change made again, not paired with the Day 161, 166 or 171 fractions; the iron stayed one piece with two bites and no Grathic word for both mercies (T062). As the year's first grain was beaten out on the flagstones, spread thin and poured back under wool, dressed and stored with no figure, the yard went around the place in the returning line where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look, a wide step and no number, and he did not join the two keepings or give either a name (T077, T098 advanced and not paid, T060/T086/T097). With Tarn neither asked about the ear or the room and the debt's stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check; with Sella her unfinished sentence still finished at the pot. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar or possession, no change of voice; forward, not closed.

- Ch 57: Days 222, 223 and 224, Grain 13–15, Thirdday to Fifthday, on stage at the wheel-shed, the cold damper, the kiln mouth and the Clay Yard on all three days. Came through with the morning's meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist had opened, not bone-coloured slip; parted dusty forearms from clean ones as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, kept them on one tray and asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, while the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack stayed entered by nobody (T009/T013/T092). Spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness, knew the full note three turns before it left his hands, with air under it and a want in it, and kept the knowing back on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them (T061/T082). Three or more men unnamed went low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on the mornings he was seen and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold; the two tellings of the room shut in the war stayed two and no third came (T035/T031, T065/T095). The burned-slate word had come over the boards four times out of Slate Row and arrived nowhere, a direction with no hand at the flame and no figure at its foot; he grew quieter whenever it went around and the household straightened around the quiet without an order, the quiet going before him into the shed the way a cold air goes before a door. The three shapings stayed sayings on the near-full tray: sings itself out early, not heard from since, gone quiet on every row. When Sella's voice went low at the hearth his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of her hand and held until the sentence reached the pot, then moved a hair's breadth and was left standing — a remembered repetition, not a change made again, not paired with the Day 161, 166 or 171 fractions; the iron stayed one piece with two bites and no Grathic word for both mercies (T062). The harvest came in with hands back in the worked strips, the first grain taken at the yard's edge and set near the hearth under wool with no figure, and the yard going around the returning-line place with a look and no number (T098 advanced and not paid, T077). With Sella his unfinished sentence still arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence; he did not look toward the bed or answer with a word. With Tarn neither asked about the ear or the room and the two silences stayed unjoined. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as on the hour it was spoken and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar, possession or change of voice; forward, not closed.

- Ch 56: Through Days 219–221, Grain 10–12, offstage on the market morning of Day 219 and at the wheel-shed on Days 220 and 221. Men unnamed came low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on the two mornings and were neither followed nor named; he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, and the two shut-room tellings stayed two with no third (T035/T031, T065/T095). He came through with meal-fine grey slate dust ground past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in a knuckle crease where a fist had opened, parted from bone-coloured slip as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, and kept dusty and clean forearms on one tray; the broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack and the space was gone around and entered by nobody (T031/T092). He spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do* and withheld its full note from men on wrong winds whose sayings — singing early, not heard from since, gone quiet on every row come the thaw — stayed three sayings about a pot with no pot lost, no fourth likeness made a cause and no name given to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188 (T061/T082, T064). The burned-slate word had come a fourth time on Grain 9 and stayed at four, a direction arrived nowhere and still not a third telling of the counting room shut in the war; the household straightened around his quiet without an order, the quiet going before him into the shed the way a cold air goes before a door. When Sella's sentence went low his bent finger went into the cold damper and held until the sentence reached the pot, then moved a hair's breadth and was left standing — a remembered repetition, not a new change, not paired with the Day 161, 166 or 171 fractions; the iron stayed one piece with two bites and no Grathic word for both mercies (T062). With Tarn neither asked about the ear or the room; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name to the slate-burners was neither given nor withdrawn. No bond paid or spoken, no new scar or possession, no change of voice.

- Ch 1: Does not appear; the wheel, the hollow seat and his objecting are held for Chapter 2.
- Ch 2: At first light stood at the wheel with slip-coloured forearms and the bent finger, told the pot the slip was too thick and it would crack at the shoulder, and went quiet when the levy was spoken of. When Sella said the boy would come under the roof he said *he's not Vail*, did not look at her, and was not answered. On the third day sat in the hollow seat as the boy was carried in, obeyed Sella's instruction about the damper before she had finished, told a pot *you'll do*, spoke once to Tarn about a rasp, and never addressed the child. Nobody wrote the foundling as Vail.

- Ch 4: Days 5 and 6. At the wheel with slip dried to old bone on both forearms and the bent little finger, speaking to the pots and not to the boy, obeying Sella before she had finished and shifting the damper before she had finished again. At dusk came in from the back room with slate dust worked into the forearms --- grey and fine, not slip --- and said *the back room's cold* to nobody, answering no question about where he had been and giving Sella nothing to take further. Garl's coat kept its salt shape on its peg and his hollow seat stayed unfilled; he did not look at the basket.

- Ch 5: Days 7--9. At the wheel with slip dried to old bone up both forearms, the bent little finger and no slate dust on Stone 13 or Stone 15; told a pot *you'll do* and the wheel *it doesn't care whose hands*, and did not look toward the basket. Shifted the damper before Sella had finished telling him the fire was low and went quieter when the levy was spoken around. Did not say the back room was cold, did not take up Sella's silence, and kept *he's not Vail* sealed while the foundling went on being carried under the roof unwritten.

- Ch 6: Days 10--14. Came in from the wheel-shed on all five days with bone-coloured slip dried to old bone up both forearms, the bent little finger and no slate dust. Told a pot *you'll do* and *the shoulder's thick; I'll thin it*, and told the wheel it did not care whose hands, only if they were steady. Shifted the damper before Sella had finished telling him the fire was low and left the end of her sentence where it was, the bent finger going in ahead of his hand. Did not say the back room was cold, did not look toward the basket or the bed, and went quieter when the levy, the uncalled debt and the reading were spoken around. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as it had been on the hour it was spoken.

- Ch 7: Days 15--24. On Stone 21 came in from the wheel-shed with bone-coloured slip and no slate dust, said she would want a thinner slip and went to the damper before Sella had finished. On Stone 22 worked at the wheel turning a pot nobody would look at twice. On Stone 23, after five clean mornings, came in with fine grey slate dust worked into the creases of his forearms past the bend of the little finger, said nothing about where he had been, told the pot *she'll want a thinner slip* and the wheel *it doesn't care whose hands; it cares if they're steady*, and took the damper ahead of Sella's instruction; a pot set down on the bench gave nothing and he did not look toward the bed. On Stone 25 the forearms were clean of dust again and he said the shoulder was thick and he would thin it. On Stone 27 spoke to a pot and the damper and let the thaw argument go over him. On Stone 28 told a pot it would do and moved the damper ahead of Sella. On Stone 29 the sweeping talk made him quieter and the yard straightened; he moved the damper before she had finished, did not say the back room was cold, and did not look toward the bed, and the boy knew he could hear the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master. On Stone 30 he had moved the damper and not said the back room was cold. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed all ten days.

- Ch 8: Days 25--30. At the wheel through the Five with bone-coloured slip dried to old bone up both forearms and the bent little finger leading at the damper, which he moved before Sella had finished; spoke to the pots and the wheel and never to the basket. No slate dust came after the Last Day, he did not say the back room was cold, did not look toward the bed or the basket, and went quieter as the Last Day sweeping talk went around; the grey lay in the corners through the baking. On Ilmen 1 worked the slip toward the thaw and the slip pits with the same forearms and the same silence. He knows the full note of a Vail pot before it leaves the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed through the year's turn and the boy went into 813 unwritten.

- Ch 9: Days 31–37. On Ilmen 2 came up from the wheel-shed with slip dried the colour of old bone up both forearms and the bent little finger leading to the damper, which he moved before Sella had finished telling him the fire was low; said *You'll do* to a pot and did not look toward the basket. Agreed with nobody about the thaw and contradicted nobody, and the yard straightened around his silence without an order. On Ilmen 4 came in from the back room with fine grey slate dust in the creases past the bend of the little finger, said nothing about where he had been, told the wheel it did not care whose hands, and went to the damper with the bent finger ahead of his hand; the full note was in a Vail pot before it left the wheel and he would not say so before a kiln-master. On Ilmen 6 told a pot it would do and moved the damper before Sella had finished. On the other mornings the forearms carried bone-coloured slip. He did not say the back room was cold and did not look toward the basket; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed through all seven days.

- Ch 10: Days 38–44. Came in from the wheel-shed at dusk on Ilmen 9 with bone-coloured slip up both forearms and moved the damper before Sella had finished; did not look toward the bed and did not ask about the woman the yard had lifted out of the brook. On Ilmen 11 came in with slip dried to old bone and no slate dust past the bend of the little finger, told a pot *You'll do* and the wheel it did not care whose hands, only if they were steady, and moved the damper ahead of the instruction with the bent finger leading. Did not say the back room was cold, agreed with nobody about the thaw, and kept *he's not Vail* sealed through all seven days.

- Ch 11: Days 45–47. Came in from the wheel-shed with slip dried to old bone up both forearms and no grey past the bend of the little finger; the bent finger went in ahead of his hand at the damper ahead of Sella's instruction on each day, and she shut her mouth and gave the rest to the pot. Told a pot *You'll do* and the wheel it did not care whose hands, only if they were steady. Said nothing about the room off the shed, did not say the back room was cold and did not look toward the bed or the basket. Garl's coat kept its peg, the hollow seat its lean and the clean dark line was not swept. The full note was in a Vail pot before it left the wheel and he would not say so before a kiln-master. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed all three days.

- Ch 12: Days 48–54. On Day 50 came in from the room off the wheel-shed with fine grey slate dust worked into the creases past his bent little finger and said nothing about where he had been; on the other mornings the forearms carried bone-coloured slip. Told a pot it would do and the wheel it did not care whose hands, only if they were steady, and knew the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel but would not say so before a kiln-master. The bent finger led at the damper ahead of Sella's instruction; he did not say the back room was cold and did not look toward the bed or the basket. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed through all seven days.

- Ch 13: Days 55--60. Came in from the wheel-shed with slip dried to old bone up both forearms and no grey past the bend of the little finger; spoke to the wheel before anyone, told a pot *You'll do*, and knew the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel without saying so before a kiln-master (T035, T061). His bent finger led at the damper ahead of Sella's instruction on the days he worked, so her sentence went to the pot; he did not say the back room was cold and did not look toward the bed or the basket. The men in that room stayed unnamed and the broken birth-slates stayed under the drying rack, gone around and entered by nobody. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed through Silt 1; the promise of Corin's name to the people who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn.

- Ch 14: Days 61–67. On Silt 4 came through the finger's-width-open door off the wheel-shed with fine grey slate dust past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; two men had come in and spoken low and he named neither. The broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, gone around and entered by nobody. Spoke to the wheel — it did not care whose hands, only if they were steady — and told a pot it would do; knew the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master. His bent finger went in ahead of his hand at the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished instruction and the household straightened around his silence. Did not say the back room was cold, did not look toward the bed or the basket; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the name ungiven.

- Ch 15: Days 68–74, Silt 9–15. On the afternoon of Silt 14 came through the finger's-width-open door off the wheel-shed with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; two men had come in since first light and spoken low and he named neither them nor the room. The broken birth-slates lay under the lowest rack where the yard went around them and entered them into nothing. Spoke to the wheel before anyone --- it did not care whose hands, only if they were steady --- told a pot it would do, knew the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master from High Kiln. His bent finger went in ahead of his hand at the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence and the household straightened around his silence. On Silt 15 came through with bone-coloured slip dried to old bone and no grey past the bend; the grey dust was the day before. Did not say the back room was cold, did not look toward the bed or the basket. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn.

- Ch 16: Days 75–77, Silt 16–18. On Day 76 came in from the wheel-shed with clean bone-coloured slip dried to old bone up both forearms; on Day 77 came through the finger's-width-open door with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the slip of the wheel. Two men had come in since first light and spoken low in the back room and he named neither them nor the room. The broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, gone around and entered by nobody. Spoke to the wheel before anyone --- it does not care whose hands, only if they are steady --- told a pot *You'll do*, knew the full note before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master from High Kiln. His bent finger went in ahead of his hand at the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence and the household straightened around his silence. Did not say the back room was cold, did not look toward the bed or the basket; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn.

- Ch 17: Days 78–84, Silt 19–25. On Silt 22 came through the finger's-width-open wheel-shed door with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; men had come in since first light and spoken low and he named neither them nor the room. The broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, gone around and entered by nobody, and he kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray without asking which slate was broken or whether it was a birth-slate. Spoke to the wheel before anyone — it does not care whose hands, only if they are steady — told a pot it would do, knew the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master from High Kiln. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low, his bent finger went in ahead of his hand at the damper and moved it, so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted. Did not say the back room was cold and did not look toward the bed or the basket. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years; the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn.

- Ch 18: Days 85–90, Silt 26–30 and Seed 1. On Silt 27 came through the finger's-width-open wheel-shed door with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; men had come in since first light and spoken low in the back room and he gave the house no name for them. The broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, gone around by the yard as wide as a pail of hot water set where a child might knock it and entered by nobody; he kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray as two states of one man's mornings and asked neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. Spoke to the wheel before anyone --- it does not care whose hands, only if they are steady --- told a pot *You'll do*, knew the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master from High Kiln. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low, his bent finger went into the damper ahead of her sentence and moved it, so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence; he did not answer her with a word and did not look at her. On Seed 1 was heard at the wheel and not seen: a low note and a word to a pot, with no figure coming out of the shed onto the yard. Did not say the back room was cold and did not look toward the bed or the basket. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years; the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn, and the hollow seat waited for his weight across the turn of the month.

- Ch 19: Days 91–93. On Seed 3 came through the finger's-width-open wheel-shed door with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; men had come in since first light and spoken low in the back room and he gave the house no name for them. The broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, gone around as wide as a pail of hot water set where a child might knock it and entered by nobody, and he kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray without asking which slate was broken or whether it was a birth-slate. Spoke to the wheel before anyone --- it did not care whose hands, only if they were steady --- told a pot *You'll do*, knew the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master from High Kiln. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low, his bent finger went into the damper ahead of her sentence, the cold iron taking the cold out of his fingertip, so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted; he did not answer her with a word and did not look at her. He read the damper as an iron gate and a bite that held warmth in and could take the hand off a man, and no word in Grathic held both mercies, and kept the reading to himself. Did not say the back room was cold and did not look toward the bed or the basket. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years; the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn.

The broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, gone around as wide as a pail of hot water set where a child might knock it and entered by nobody, and he kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray without asking which slate was broken or whether it was a birth-slate. Spoke to the wheel before anyone --- it does not care whose hands, only if they are steady --- told a pot *You'll do*, knew the full note before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master from High Kiln. Put his bent finger into the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the end of it arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence, reading the iron as the bite that holds and takes with no Grathic word for both and keeping the reading to himself. Heard at the wheel on Seed 11 and not seen. Did not say the back room was cold and did not look toward the bed or the basket; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the foundling's name was neither given nor withdrawn.

- Ch 22: Day 105, Seed 16, the only day he is on stage. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of her sentence, the iron taking the cold out of his fingertip, so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted; he did not answer with a word and did not look at her. - Ch 24: Day 111, Seed 22, the only day he is on stage. Men came up the Clay Yard before first grey with their collars high and spoke low in the finger's-width-open back room; he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, and came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel. He kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray without asking which slate had been broken or whether it was a birth-slate, and the broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, gone around and entered by nobody. Spoke to the wheel before anyone — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master come up from High Kiln who would call it early and make something of it. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of her sentence, the iron taking the cold out of his fingertip and giving a little back in reverse of the core, so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted; he did not answer with a word and did not look at her. **New:** Tarn's broom checked half a beat on the flagstones behind his standing finger and then went on without a word or a turn of the head; neither man named the other's silence and the loan that can hear was not called, but the equality under the roof moved by that much for the first time. He read the iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and can take the hand off a man, with no Grathic word for both mercies, and filed it as an object joined to no kiln, exact and worth nothing. Did not look toward the bed or into the basket; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. Offstage on Seed 21 and 23. No new scar, possession or change of voice.

- Ch 25: Day 114, Seed 25, the only day he is on stage; offstage on Seed 24 and 26. Men came up the Clay Yard before first grey with their collars high and went into the finger's-width-open back room; the low voices did not carry to the edge of the light where Sella had set the basket. He gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, and came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel; he kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray without asking which slate had been broken or whether it was a birth-slate. The broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, gone around as wide as a pail of hot water set where a child might knock it and entered by nobody. Spoke to the wheel before anyone — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master come up from High Kiln who would call it early and make something of it. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low, his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of her sentence, the iron taking the cold out of his fingertip, so that the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted; he did not answer with a word and did not look at her. He read the iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and can take the hand off a man, with no Grathic word for both mercies, and filed it as an object joined to no kiln, exact and worth nothing. **New:** the debt's stroke, which had checked half a beat behind his standing finger on Seed 22, went on over the flagstones without the check now, the stones still keeping the place where it had stopped; neither man named the other's silence and the loan that can hear was not called. Did not look toward the bed or into the basket; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. No new scar, possession or change of voice.

- Ch 26: Day 117, Seed 28, the only day he is on stage; offstage on Seed 27, 29 and 30. Men came up the Clay Yard before first grey with their collars high and went low-voiced into the finger's-width-open room off the wheel-shed; he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, and came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel. The dust had been worked inward into the creases, the way grit works into a knee; he kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray without asking which slate had been broken or whether it was a birth-slate, and the broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, gone around as wide as a pail of hot water set where a child might knock it and entered by nobody. Spoke to the wheel before anyone — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome in it and no unkindness either, knew the full note of a Vail pot before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master come up from High Kiln who would call it early and make something of it. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low, his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of her sentence, the iron taking the cold out of his fingertip and giving a little back in reverse of the core, so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted; he did not answer with a word and did not look at her. He read the iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and the bite that can take the hand off a man, one piece of iron and two mercies with no Grathic word for both, and filed it as an object worth nothing, joined to no firing. **The debt's stroke, which had checked half a beat behind his standing finger on Seed 22, went on over the flagstones without the check, the stones still keeping the place where it had stopped; neither man named the other's silence and the loan that can hear was not called.** Did not look toward the bed or into the basket; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as the hour it was spoken three years ago and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. No new scar, possession or change of voice.

- Ch 27: Day 121, Reed 2, Secondday, the only day he is on stage; offstage on Reed 1 and 3. Men came up the Clay Yard before first grey with their collars high and went low-voiced into the finger's-width-open room off the wheel-shed; he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, and came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not the bone-coloured slip of the wheel. He kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate; the broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, gone around and entered by nobody. Spoke to the wheel before anyone — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master from High Kiln. When Sella had not finished telling him the fire was low his bent finger went into the cold damper ahead of her sentence, the iron taking the cold out of his fingertip, so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted; he did not answer with a word and did not look at her. He read the iron as the bite that holds and the bite that can take the hand off a man, one piece of iron and two mercies with no Grathic word for both, and filed it as an object worth nothing, joined to no kiln. The debt's stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check, the stones keeping the shallow bright patch where it had stopped, and neither man named the other's silence. Did not look toward the bed or the basket; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. No new scar, possession or change of voice.

- Ch 28: Day 124, Reed 5, the only day he is on stage; offstage on Reed 4 and 6. Men unnamed came before first grey with collars high into the finger's-width-open back room; he gave them no name, did not say the room was cold, and came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger, not bone-coloured slip, keeping dusty and clean forearms on one tray. Spoke to the wheel before anyone, told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note before it left the wheel and withheld it from a High Kiln kiln-master. Put his bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the end of it arrived at the pot, and read the double bite as an object joined to no kiln with no Grathic word for both mercies. Tarn's stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check behind his standing finger; neither man named the other's silence, and he did not ask about the near-saying that night. Did not look toward the bed or the basket; *he's not Vail* sealed and the promise to the slate-burners neither given nor withdrawn. No new scar, possession or change of voice.

- Ch 29: Day 127, Reed 8, the only day he is on stage; offstage on Reed 7 and 9. Men unnamed came up the Clay Yard before first grey with collars high and went low-voiced into the finger's-width-open back room; he gave the house no name for them, did not say the room was cold, and came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger, not bone-coloured slip, keeping dusty and clean forearms on one tray and asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. Spoke to the wheel before anyone — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note before it left the wheel and withheld it from a High Kiln kiln-master who would have called it early. Put his bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing; the boy read the double bite as an object joined to no kiln with no Grathic word for both mercies. Tarn's stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check behind his standing finger; neither man named the other's silence and he did not ask about the near-saying when it came a second time that night. The broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, entered by nobody; the two shut-room tellings kept beside the iron with no third. Did not look toward the bed or the basket; *he's not Vail* sealed and the promise to the slate-burners neither given nor withdrawn. No new scar, possession or change of voice.

- Ch 30: Day 130, Reed 11, the only day he is on stage; offstage on Reed 10 and 12. Last seen at the going of the light come through from the shed with bone-coloured slip dried to old bone on both forearms. Earlier that day, before first grey, three or four unnamed men came up the Clay Yard with their collars high and spoke low in the finger's-width-open back room; he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, and came out with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bend of the little finger, not bone-coloured slip, keeping dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. Spoke to the wheel before anyone — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note before it left the wheel and withheld it from a High Kiln kiln-master who would have called it early and made something of it. Put his bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing; the boy read the double bite as an object joined to no kiln with no Grathic word for both mercies (T062). Tarn's stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check behind his standing finger; neither man named the other's silence and he did not ask about the near-saying when it came a third time that night. The broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, entered by nobody; the two shut-room tellings kept beside the iron with no third (T065/T095). Did not look toward the bed or the basket; *he's not Vail* sealed and the promise to the slate-burners neither given nor withdrawn. No new scar, possession or change of voice.

- Ch 31: Day 133, Reed 14, the only day he is on stage; offstage on Reed 13 and 15. Last seen at the going of the light come through from the shed with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger and under the nails, not bone-coloured slip. Earlier that day, before first grey, three or four unnamed men came up the Clay Yard with their collars high and spoke low in the finger's-width-open back room; he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, came through with the dust, kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate. Spoke to the wheel before anyone — *It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady* — told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness, knew its full note before it left the wheel and withheld it from a High Kiln kiln-master who said the pot was singing before its time. Put his bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing; the boy read the double bite as an object joined to no kiln with no Grathic word for both mercies (T062). Tarn's stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check behind his standing finger; neither man named the other's silence, and he did not ask about the near-saying when it came a **fourth** time that night. The broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack, entered by nobody; the two tellings of the shut counting room kept beside the iron with no third (T065/T095). Did not look toward the bed or the basket; *he's not Vail* sealed and the promise to the slate-burners neither given nor withdrawn. No new scar, possession or change of voice.

- Ch 32: Day 136, Reed 17, on stage at the wheel-shed, the damper, the Clay Yard and the going of the light under the Vail roof; offstage on Days 135 and 137. Last seen coming through from the shed with fine grey slate dust still on both forearms past the bent little finger, telling a pot *You'll do* and not asking about the ear. Before first grey three or four unnamed men had gone low into the back room and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold. Kept dusty and clean forearms on one tray, asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), knew a pot's full note three turns before it left the wheel and withheld it from a High Kiln kiln-master who said it was singing before its time and that a pot that sings early generally sings itself out early. Put his bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing; the boy read the double bite as an object joined to no kiln. Did not look toward the bed or the basket; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the name to the slate-burners was neither given nor withdrawn.

The boy read one piece of iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no Grathic word for both mercies, filed as an object joined to no kiln, with the two shut-room tellings beside it and no third (T062, T065/T095). No new scar or possession, no change of voice.

- Ch 35: Day 145, Reed 26, on stage at the wheel-shed, the damper and the Clay Yard; offstage on Days 144 and 146. Men unnamed came before first grey with collars high and went low into the finger's-width-open back room; he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, and came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger and under the nails, not bone-coloured slip. Kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray, asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do*, and knew its full note before it left the wheel and withheld it from a High Kiln kiln-master who said it was singing before its time and would make something of it. Before Sella had finished telling him the fire was low his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing, so the end of her instruction arrived at the pot and the household straightened around his silence; the boy read the bite that holds a vessel whole and the bite that can take the hand off a man, with no Grathic word for both mercies, filed as an object joined to no kiln (T035/T031/T062). Tarn's stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check behind his standing finger, and he did not ask about the ear when Tarn brought it an **eighth** time to the word that would call the loan that night; Tarn did not ask about the room and the two silences stayed unjoined (T036/T076, T065/T095). The broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack and the two tellings of the shut counting room stood beside the damper with no third. Did not look toward the bed or the basket; *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. No new scar or possession, no change of voice.

- Ch 39: Day 161, Tallow 12, on stage at the wheel-shed, the cold damper, the flagstones and the Clay Yard; offstage on Days 160 and 162. Three or four men went low into the finger’s-width-open back room before first grey and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, no third telling of the shut counting room (T035/T031, T065/T095). He gave nobody a word about the waiting, did not look toward the basket and did not ask about the ear; the two silences stayed unjoined and the loan uncalled. *He’s not Vail* sealed, promise of the foundling’s name ungiven. No new scar, possession or change of voice.

The boy filed it beside the Day 161 fraction at the damper and the Day 166 held beat beneath the loaf shelf — three held changes on three places, not a pair, doctrine or rule (T036/T076, T062, T100, T061/T082).
