- Ch 54: Days 213, 214 and 215, Grain 4–6, Fourthday to Sixthday, on stage at the wheel-shed, the cold damper, the kiln mouth, the drying racks and the Clay Yard on all three days. Three or more men unnamed went low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on all three mornings and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold; the two shut-room tellings stayed two with no third (T035/T031, T065/T095). Came through with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist had opened, not bone-coloured slip; parted dusty forearms from clean ones as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, kept them on one tray and asked neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate; the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack stayed entered by nobody (T009/T013/T092). **The sayings:** on Day 214 a kiln-hand come up from High Kiln on a wrong wind said the pot that was singing before its time last month had not been heard from since; Jorra, who had known that pot's full note three turns before it left his hands and holds the nearly-full answer with air under it and a want in it, let the saying stand as a saying and gave no name to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188, made no lesson of the green on the wind, and no pot was lost on the fifth morning of Grain (T061/T082 advanced and unpaid, T064, T035/T031, T077). The sweet edge of lead glaze came up off High Kiln on the wrong wind and went out on it, taken as a direction and not a second edge paid. On Day 215 the burned-slate word came over the boards a third time from Slate Row, spoken over the boards and not to the basket on the same breath as the price of barley and the weather, with no owner, room, hand at the flame or reason; he grew quieter whenever it went around and the household straightened around the quiet without an order, the quiet going before him into the shed the way cold air goes before a door, and the word stayed a direction arrived nowhere and not a third telling of the room shut in the war (T035/T031/T065/T095 advanced, not paid). Spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness, and when Sella's voice went low his bent finger went into the cold damper and held until the sentence reached the pot, then moved a hair's breadth and was left standing — a remembered repetition, not a change made again and not stood up with the three held changes into a rule (T062). *He's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn; the two silences stayed unjoined and the iron stayed one piece with two bites, joined to no kiln. No new scar, possession or change of voice. Forward, not closed.

- Ch 55: Through Days 216–218, Grain 7–9, the wheel and the back room keep their old shape and the sayings about a pot stand as sayings about a pot. Three or more men unnamed went low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey on all three mornings and he gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold; the two shut-room tellings stayed two with no third (T035/T031, T065/T095). He came through with meal-fine grey slate dust ground small as meal past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist had opened, not bone-coloured slip; he parted dusty forearms from clean ones as a surgeon parts clot from vessel, kept them on one tray and asked neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, while the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack stayed entered by nobody (T009/T013/T092). **The sayings:** on Grain 8 a kiln-hand come up from High Kiln on a wrong wind with a barrow said over the boards that there was a pot gone quiet on every row come the thaw. Jorra, who knew the pot's full note three turns before it left his hands and holds the nearly-full answer with air under it and a want in it, let the saying stand as a saying, gave no name to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188, made no lesson of the green on the wind and lost no pot (T061/T082 advanced and unpaid, T064, T035/T031, T077). The sweet edge of lead glaze came up off High Kiln on the wrong wind and went out on it, a direction and not a second edge paid. Three shapings of one saying stood on the near-full tray — sung before its time, not heard from since, gone quiet on every row — with a finger between them and no finger laid across, and no fourth likeness stood up to call itself a cause. On Grain 9 the burned-slate word came over the boards a fourth time from Slate Row, spoken over the boards and not to the basket on the same breath as the price of barley and the weather, with no owner, room, hand at the flame or reason; he grew quieter whenever it went around and the household straightened around the quiet without an order, the quiet going before him into the shed the way cold air goes before a door, and the word was a fourth direction arrived nowhere and still not a third telling of the room shut in the war (T035/T031/T065/T095 advanced, not paid). He spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome in it and no unkindness, and when Sella's voice went low and unfinished at the hearth his bent finger went into the cold damper ahead of her hand and held until the sentence reached *feeding* and the end was given to the pot, then moved the tongue a hair's breadth and was left standing — a remembered repetition, not a change made again, not stood up with the Day 161, 166 or 171 changes into a rule (T062). The iron stayed one piece with two bites, the holding and the taking, with no word in Grathic for both mercies, joined to no kiln and entered in no slate. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as on the hour it was spoken, and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn; the claim gathered interest in the rooms he went into and he did not pay it and did not refuse it, which in this district is a way of keeping a thing alive. He did not ask about the ear and Tarn did not ask about the room; the two silences stayed unjoined. No new scar, possession or change of voice. Forward, not closed.

- Ch 64: Days 243, 244 and 245, Smoke 4–6, Fourthday to Sixthday, on stage at the wheel-shed edge, the cold damper, the kiln mouth, the drying racks and the Clay Yard on all three days. The three shapings of the pot saying stood as sayings — sings itself out early, not heard from since, gone quiet on every row come the thaw — and on Smoke 6 a wrong wind off High Kiln brought the sweet edge of lead glaze over the boards, touched the fold and went out on the wind that brought it; he let one of the sayings stand again as a saying, made no lesson of the green, lost no pot and gave no name to what ailed the man carried out on Day 188 (T064 held, T077). The burned-slate word held at four tellings out of Slate Row, still a direction arrived nowhere and still not a third telling of the counting room shut in the war; the row question stood on its seventh place with four askings, no owner, room, hand at the kiln, cause, witness or row named, and he did not claim it, supply a witness or make a cause of it (T101 carried). When Sella's sentence went low and unfinished at the hearth his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper ahead of it, took the cold out of the fingertip, held until the end of the sentence arrived at the pot, moved a hair's breadth and was left standing — a remembered repetition and not a change made again, paired with no other fraction. He did not ask about the ear while Tarn did not ask about the room, and the two silences stayed unjoined. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as on the hour it was spoken, and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. The dressed grain stayed covered under wool with no figure and the returning-line place went around with a look and no number; the ninth record-first hand seen at the well stayed on its own tray in the boy's file and was joined to nothing here. No new scar, possession or change of voice, no bond paid or spoken.

On Smoke 14 a pot set down wrong at the racks, out of a woman's shadow and near the warm side of the wall, gave its high thin note before its time; the woman swept around it and said something practical about the slip, and he made no lesson of the sound, no fourth likeness a cause and no name for what ailed the man carried out on Day 188, and no pot was lost. It cares if they're steady* — told a pot *You'll do* in a voice with no welcome and no unkindness, knew its full note three turns before it left his hands with air under it and a want in it, and withheld the knowing on the near-full tray beside the hearings and never among them, advanced and not paid (T061/T082). He did not ask about the ear while Tarn did not ask about the room, and the two silences stayed unjoined. *He's not Vail* stayed sealed as tightly as a lid nobody had tried to lift in three years, and the promise of the boy's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn. No new scar or possession, no change of voice, no bond paid or spoken.

## Secrets (private)

1. He has broken three birth-slates in the last two years and buried the pieces under the drying rack. He has never told the Broken Slates, because he wants them to believe he is still deciding.
2. He knows where the district's second set of birth notes is kept — Alda Reed's tin under a floorboard — and has not burned it because he is afraid of what is written there about the year of the flower.
3. He has promised a slate with Corin's name on it to the Broken Slates. If the boy has no house, his name is easy to give; it is the one thing Jorra has that costs him nothing except, perhaps, the boy.
4. He does not want the wheel at all. He wants to leave Low Kiln, which he would say is impossible, which is what he says whenever he is asked anything real.
5. The bent finger came from the wheel, not from a fight. He tells people it was a fight because a fight sounds more like him.

## Plans (private)

Make him dangerous by the smallness of the act, not the size: he will hand Corin's name to people who burn slates, and he will do it while believing he is protecting the house. He is not a villain and will never be given a villain's speech; he is a young man with a wound and a wheel and a grievance that has been lent to him.

He becomes the one who tells Corin, years later and cruelly, that the woman who bore him was Dessa Silt and that she has been dead since the Slip Brook took her — a truth used as a weapon because he cannot say the thing he actually means, which is *I was here first*. He stays in Low Kiln, keeper of the wheel, a man who has never said which side he is on until the day he says it and it costs him the house. His arc is the saga's first proof that a person can be both wounded and wrong without being either one exclusively.
